It addresses the cosmic tension between the ministering angels—who believe the Divine Presence belongs only in the spiritual realms—and the physical world below.</meta>

# Commentary

**Left Column**

# The watchmen who go about the city found me; 
# “Have you seen him whom my soul loves?”

# Scarcely had I passed from them,
# Until I found him whom my soul loves;
# I held him, and would not let him go, until I had brought him
# Into my mother’s house, and into the chamber of her that conceived me.

# I adjure you, O daughters of Jerusalem,
# By the gazelles, or by the hinds of the field, 
# That you stir not up, nor awake love,
# Until it please.

# Who is this that comes up from the wilderness like pillars 
# of smoke, perfumed with myrrh and frankincense, 
# with all the powders of the merchant?

<column-break/>

**Right Column**

This has already been explained according to the first method of interpretation. 
And according to the second method, it means: because I sought him whom my soul loves and I did not find him, "the watchmen who go about the city found me" <note>Song of Songs 3:3</note> to remember him. And the change—if he were to be a King over all—and they bore me entirely. And he also placed a balm forever when I sought him whom my soul loves, so that they might mend their deeds and turn to be cleaved unto Him, in order to bring healing and a cure for the sickness of their exile. This is just as the Prophet Ezekiel <note>original: *Yechezkel HaNavi*</note> used to rebuke them and bring them near, as it is written in the tradition.

And behold: "Scarcely had I passed from them" <note>Song of Songs 3:4</note>—meaning from those aforementioned watchmen—"until I found him whom my soul loves." This refers to the Holy One, Blessed be He <note>original: *HaShem Yitbarakh*</note>. For after the passing of the kingdom of the infidels <note>original: *Kefarim*</note> and the power of the kings of Media and Persia, the Redemption began. And this is the meaning of "until I found him whom my soul loves." And even if sins caused a delay and there was a cancellation in the building of the Temple—as is explained in the Book of Ezra—nevertheless, "I held him and would not let him go until I had brought him into my mother’s house," etc., which refers to the building of the Holy Temple <note>term: *Beit HaMikdash* — the central place of worship in Jerusalem</note>, when "the crown was restored to its former glory" <note>a traditional Hebrew idiom for restoring a former state of holiness or sovereignty</note>.

This speaks against the angels, in whose eyes the dwelling of the Divine Presence <note>term: *Shekhinah* — the feminine, indwelling aspect of God's presence on earth</note> among those in the lower world was evil. As is known, because of this, the verse says: "How majestic is Your name in all the earth!" <note>Psalms 8:2</note>. According to the truth of the intellect, judgment would be on their side, for they are spiritual and intellectual beings, and for them it is fitting for the Divine Presence to dwell—not here among the lower beings, who are physical and corporeal. This matter is always evil in their eyes, and if they find a place to disagree or to prosecute <note>term: *Kitrug* — spiritual accusation or prosecution in the heavenly court</note> due to a lack of merit, they will open a door and not maintain their rest and benefit.

Therefore, King Solomon adjured them, saying: "I adjure you, O daughters of Jerusalem" <note>Song of Songs 3:5</note>—referring to the high level of the angels—"by the gazelles <note>original: *Tzeva'ot*</note> or by the hinds of the field," as our Sages of blessed memory interpreted: "by the Heavenly Hosts <note>original: *Tzva'ot*</note> and the Will." And I have adjured them, or perhaps by the merit of the Covenant of Circumcision <note>term: *Brit Milah*</note>... "from the wilderness." And the oath is: "if you stir up" prosecution and judgment, "or if you awake love"—meaning the matter of the love that the Holy One, Blessed be He, has for Israel—to prosecute against it. Rather, leave it be "until it please" to be among the lower beings, and do not disagree with it. This interpretation applies to both methods: whether concerning the building of the First Temple or the building of the Second Temple...

Solomon, peace be upon him, further said, to uphold his oath to the spiritual angels so that they would accept it: even though he diminished the merit of Israel in three parts... nevertheless, they have a collective merit for all of them...

<summary>This page interprets the soul's search for God as the historical transition from the Babylonian exile to the rebuilding of the Second Temple. It portrays the biblical "adjuration" as King Solomon's warning to angels not to interfere with God's love for the physical, "corporeal" people of Israel.</summary>

# Song of Songs

Furthermore, it is possible to deepen the meaning and draw down power from the place where the three Patriarchs—Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob—find refuge. These three are the attributes of **Kindness**, **Judgment**, and **Mercy**, which are symbolized by silver, gold, and the "translator" <note>The Hebrew text reads *Targeman* (translator), though this is likely a mystical play on *Argaman* (purple fabric), which typically represents the Patriarch Jacob and the attribute of Beauty/Mercy in the Tabernacle's colors.</note>. The binding of their worlds is the essence of Divine Unity. Therefore, you shall merit to be "steeped in love from the daughters of Jerusalem," and this is easy to understand.

## Go forth and gaze, daughters of Zion, at King Solomon, at the crown with which his mother crowned him on the day of his wedding, and on the day of his heart’s joy.

He <note>the author of the Song</note> spoke through the **Holy Spirit** <note>original: *Ruach HaKodesh*</note> as one speaking to all of us with strength: "Go and let us see." This means one should strive to remove oneself from the boundaries of materiality <note>original: *Homer*</note>. By doing so, you will merit to perceive and see, for your eyes will be opened. You will see the cleaving and the union of the **Divine Presence** <note>original: *Shekhinah*</note> through that which King Solomon made—the **Holy Temple** <note>original: *Beit HaMikdash*</note>. 

This is the meaning of "at King Solomon"—meaning, through the actions of King Solomon, and specifically in the union of the **Congregation of Zion** <note>original: *Knesset Zion*</note>. This union occurs through a likeness and reflection drawn from the refinement of their physical matter, achieved through the performance of the commandments <note>original: *Mitzvot*</note> and the fulfillment of the Torah. 

This is also the meaning of "daughters of Zion" <note>original: *Bnot Tzion*</note>. Our Sages, of blessed memory, interpreted this as "the distinguished sons" <note>The author uses a Hebrew pun where *Bnot* (daughters) is read as *Banim* (sons) and *Tzion* (Zion) is read as *Metzuyanim* (distinguished/marked)</note>. These are the ones who are marked for Me through specific commandments, such as the hair-cutting laws, circumcision, and the like. They are joined to the totality of the Torah and the commandments, through which a person refines their physical substance and body, thus meriting to be cleaved to their God. 

Regarding the phrase "at the crown with which his mother crowned him," this means that the **Divine Presence** crowned him because of what he bound together through the work of his hands. He acted, received, and achieved that which he sought, so that the Divine Presence would be bound to the lower worlds. This indicates the high level and greatness of King Solomon. His "mother" who crowns him is the **Divine Presence**. 

"On the day of his wedding" refers to the compassion of his kingdom, for he was crowned with wisdom and knowledge more than all other creatures, as it is written: "And he was wiser than all men" <note>1 Kings 5:11</note>. "And on the day of his heart’s joy" refers to the matter of the Holy Temple, where he bound the Divine Presence through his handiwork. All this relates to the "crown of the diadem of wisdom," showing he was worthy of the ultimate purpose of building the Temple because he was close to God and cleaved to Him. God bestowed upon him a "mighty holy spirit" <note>original: *Ruach Hasin Kadishin*</note> of wisdom and knowledge above all the world’s creatures. Who is like him, a wise man who can speak such elegant words regarding the Lord dwelling in the midst of the Children of Israel?

The "gift of kindness" mentioned after this verse refers to the words of the daughters of Jerusalem who blessed us. They were elevated by this because when they heard the fame of Solomon’s words and his nature—as I have written—they agreed with his words. They said to Israel that many have sought to free their souls from the filth of materiality, which is humbled through the fulfillment of the Torah and the commandments. Through this, they will merit to find constant favor in that which King Solomon did.

## Behold, you are beautiful, my love; behold, you are beautiful. Your eyes are like doves behind your veil; your hair is like a flock of goats streaming down from Mount Gilead.

This is the response of the Blessed Name <note>God</note> regarding the "business dealings" <note>Likely referring to the intellectual exchange</note> that King Solomon had regarding the riddles of the **Queen of Sheba**. People had challenged him, but God saw that Solomon was arguing in favor of Israel, citing the merits they possessed which made them worthy of the Divine Presence dwelling among them. 

Because of this, God’s words come as if confirming Solomon’s words, saying: "Behold, you are beautiful, my love"—meaning you are beautiful, pure, and clean, worthy of being My beloved in union and attachment. And this [the soul/Israel] is worthy for the Presence to dwell within you.

# Commentary

Therefore, they are always "holders of the sword" etc. Their war is in their hands to stand against the adversary. And these are "learned in war"—meaning they are experts in the war against the **Evil Inclination** <term>Yetzer HaRa: the innate human drive toward selfishness or sin</term> and heroes who overcome its persuasions, smooth talk, cunning, and deceits. This is especially true in the matter of "guarding the sign of the holy covenant" <note>referring to the maintenance of moral and sexual purity</note> so as not to defile it; for this is the essential principle in distancing the side of impurity and its forces, so they do not approach a person or rest upon them. This is the meaning of "each man with his sword upon his thigh because of the terror in the night," which refers to the forces of **Judgment** <note>Din: the divine attribute of rigor and restriction</note> and the side of impurity.

He returned and explained concerning the **Holy Temple** <note>Beit HaMikdash</note>:

# King Solomon made himself a palanquin 
# of the wood of Lebanon.

Its being a dwelling and fit for the resting of the **Divine Presence** <term>Shekhinah: the feminine, indwelling aspect of God</term> is like a house for a wedding or a canopy for a groom and bride. Although it was made of the wood of Lebanon, which is physical and material and seemingly lacks the readiness to be a dwelling for the Divine Presence, he nevertheless made it in a way that it was fit for this. This was achieved through the **intention** <term>Kavannah: spiritual focus or meditative directedness</term> and thought during the craftsmanship, dedicated to the sanctity of the Heavenly Temple. In every part of its parts and every vessel of its vessels, they intended for the supernal part or vessel corresponding to it and in its likeness. In this way, they would draw down the supernal flow and spirituality into it. Thus, the lower, physical object would acquire holiness, purity, and spirituality through this process, making it worthy of the resting of the Divine Presence. 

This was the wisdom of **Bezalel** <note>the master craftsman of the Tabernacle in the desert</note> and the other wise men of the Tabernacle: besides the physical labor on the parts and vessels of the Tabernacle, the essence of their wisdom lay in these intentions to bring down supernal spirituality into the lower realms. This is the meaning of the verse "to devise artistic designs to work in gold" etc. <note>Exodus 31:4</note>. This is the rectification and the making that Solomon performed; as will be further explained, it was prepared in a manner fit for the essence of the Temple, even though it exists in a physical and material world.

And he intends to explain: "even though its pillars were silver..."

# He made its pillars of silver, its base of gold, 
# its seat of purple, its interior inlaid with love 
# from the daughters of Jerusalem.

Even though it uses silver, which is a type of metal, and likewise its base of gold and its seat of purple—all of which are physical and material types—precious as they may be, nonetheless "its interior was inlaid with love." This means that within the Temple, the fire of love was always burning, lit and flaming from the side of the service performed within it. This service is more precious and exalted than the work of the **angels** <note>who are referred to here as "the daughters of Jerusalem"</note>. 

The matter is clear: at the Altar of Sacrifices, the forces of Judgment would be separated and removed to a place designated for them, and the path would be cleared and made vacant to ascend on high. In this way, the supernal lights were unified through the sacrifices, and the divine flow descends to all the worlds, as explained in the **Book of Splendor** <term>Zohar: the foundational text of Jewish mysticism</term> in many places. According to this, even the angels receive great benefit through the service performed in the Holy Temple, something they cannot merit through their own power at all. This is the meaning of "its interior inlaid with love from the daughters of Jerusalem"—the phrase "from the daughters of Jerusalem" here denotes the advantage bestowed upon the angels. 

Alternatively, it may mean "he made its pillars of silver" in the way we explained "he made himself a palanquin" etc. The intention is that the silver, gold, and purple were sufficient to make its pillars, its base, and its seat—even though they are physical—so that they would be fit to serve this palanquin. He prepared this so that the physical would become spiritual and relate to the dwelling of the Divine Presence, through the intention at the time of its making, as we have said.

It also highlights how the Temple service benefits even the angelic realms by refining the forces of Judgment.</summary>

<meta>The previous page discussed the "sixty warriors" surrounding the bed of Solomon, interpreted as spiritual guardians against the Evil Inclination. It also introduced the construction of the Temple as a physical dwelling for the Divine Presence made through holy intention.</meta>

The gaze and the roaming of the eye that pushes toward and looks at evil becomes defiled and turns away, just as in a moment of wrath. God had mercy on her <note>referring to the soul</note> in the womb while she was in this world; He remembers her when she is stripped from the body, for the portion of the soul was lacking. He gave power and sight to the eye, but it became blemished, damaged, and darkened. The Holy One, blessed be He, surrounded it with a wall <note>the eyelids or eyelashes</note> and a protective barrier <note>original: "perachlit" - likely meaning an advocate or shield</note> for the eyes and said, **"Your eyes are doves."** This means that the eyes should have no blemish at all from the perspective of a forbidden gaze that integrates with an evil thing. For the eyes are like rivers flowing from the brain and a pure fountain; they should choose without any dimness or stagnation at all, and not bring about a loss of strength or a sudden snatching of light from their life. Rather, on the contrary, they are the bringers of light to the luminaries of the body's limbs and even to the entire world when they are used for the purpose of a **commandment** <term>Mitzvah: a religious duty or good deed</term> and the study of the **Torah** <term>Torah: the body of Jewish divine law and teachings</term>. This is the secret of **"Your eyes are doves,"** as the Sages, of blessed memory, said: "Just as this dove brought light to the world" <note>a reference to the dove in the story of Noah's Ark</note>, etc. 

And since the hair is also a tool for the strengthening of the **Evil Inclination** <term>Yetzer HaRa: the innate human drive toward selfishness</term> when a person beautifies himself with it—as the Sages, of blessed memory, said regarding **Joseph the Righteous**, that he would "curl his hair" <note>an idiom for vanity or self-adornment</note>, etc. Also, regarding the voice, its power passes through the arrangements of **Gevurah** <term>Gevurah: the Divine attribute of Strength and Restraint</term> and came before **Shimon the Just** <note>a High Priest in the Second Temple period</note> to understand the cause. And he said: **"from behind your veil"** <note>Song of Songs 4:1</note>—meaning from the arrangement of the hair. It was revealed that this could be a cause for the strengthening of the Evil Inclination, but it is meant to be internal, for ornaments within the one bound by them. This is the path of humility regarding the hair: it is like a flock of goats that streamed down from Mount Gilead. This means that after they were shorn and removed from the mountain, no trace of them remained there at all. In the same way, the hair should not be a tool for harm; and if there is a potential in it to cause harm, it should be treated 
->with mercy:<-

<margin>It is known that the general principle etc. is that there is great kindness in the eye from one side, while from the second side, they are a vessel that can be blocked to speak impurity and be led by forbidden thoughts, as they elaborated in the **Zohar** <term>Zohar: the foundational work of Jewish mysticism</term> on the portion of **Shemini** <note>Leviticus 9:1–11:47</note>.</margin>

# Your teeth are like a flock of shorn ewes 
# that have come up from the washing, 
# all of them bear twins, 
# and not one among them is bereaved.

And for anyone who ruins the smile and the external aspects through their deficiencies, they become attached to the **Other Side** <term>Sitra Achra: the Kabbalistic term for the realm of evil or impurity</term>. He casts out his soul and his body, and a spirit of impurity rests upon him. He sees within his body that he has no portion in the Upper Holiness, and he does not come from its hidden places, nor does he cling to it, etc. This is sufficient as an explanation for the verse: "All the toil of man is for his mouth" <note>Ecclesiastes 6:7</note>. And see what the Master said regarding the reason why a person is judged in that world: all the voices are recorded for judgment. Anyone who is foolish in that world and is cut off from the existence of the world because of his mouth—it is because his mouth did not guard him. He ruins and closes himself off from the soul, and does not cling to the **Book of Life** <note>Sefer HaChaim</note> on the right side. Furthermore, the soul will never reach its perfection in judgment for all eternity; it does not rise and is not completed to receive its reward forever, because it became defiled and attached to the Other Side. 

Until here <note>end of the quote/reference</note>. Thus, it is clear before us how far the depth of the impure complex reaches, and the depth of the blemish that extends to the body itself. The vessels of the face, which are the tools of the senses, are susceptible to blemishes, and a "permanent blemish" is mentioned regarding them if they are blocked by impure thoughts. Conversely, through kindness, the radiance of the face breaks through and he is led by it; behold, his face is pure and clear like milk without any blemish at all. Through kindness, there will be the greatest caution, as **Ezekiel** <note>the Prophet</note> said: "No loathsome meat has ever entered my mouth" <note>Ezekiel 4:14</note>, following the path explained in the **Talmud** <term>Gemara/Talmud: the central text of Rabbinic Judaism</term>. And also to bring oneself close through that which is permitted to us—that is, to make all his days like festivals. They have the likeness of whiteness and grace, for there is also beauty in order, emptiness, and repair. This is the secret of **"Your teeth are like a flock of shorn ewes,"** which are in rows of eight and eight, for all of them!

It warns that misusing these "vessels of the face" for vanity or impurity attaches the soul to the "Other Side" and prevents spiritual perfection.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the allegorical interpretation of the Song of Songs, specifically moving from the general protection of the soul (the "sixty warriors" and the "wall" mentioned previously) to a detailed breakdown of the beloved's features, interpreted as different classes of people and spiritual virtues within the community of Israel.</meta>

-># Commentary<-

From the aspects of His goodness, as he goes on to specify: First and foremost, **"Behold, you are beautiful,"** meaning your very essence and your capacity for containment are undoubtedly characterized by beauty and purity. For they are the "seed blessed by the Lord," refined and pure, the "threefold cord" <note>A reference to Ecclesiastes 4:12, here applied to the merit of the three Patriarchs: Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob</note> of **Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob**. From this perspective, there is beauty in the entirety of the "Wall" <note>The "Wall" serves as a metaphor for the collective strength and structure of the Jewish people</note> in that it possesses the power to cause her to cleave to God, may He be blessed, and to her source, she who is "My beloved," as we have said. This is the meaning of **"Behold, you are beautiful."** Furthermore, **"Your eyes are doves,"** and as it says, "Your eyes are doves... your hair... your teeth..." and so on.

In truth, the **Midrash** <term>Midrash: ancient rabbinic commentaries that use parables and stories to explain the Bible</term> and the **Targum** <term>Targum: the traditional Aramaic translation and expansion of the biblical text</term> and other commentators, of blessed memory, agreed that these verses refer to the various parts and powers of the "Wall" and the community, which are ranked one above the other; the scriptures speak in praise of these specific parts. 

According to the **Targum**, **"Your eyes"** refers to the leaders of the congregation and the **Sanhedrin** <term>Sanhedrin: the supreme high court of ancient Israel</term>, who bring light to the world. **"Your hair"** refers to the rest of the people, who are all righteous like the sons of **Jacob**, who gathered stones and made a heap at Mount Gilead. He said **"Your teeth"** refers to the **Priests and Levites** <note>The classes of people dedicated to Temple service</note> who offer the sacrifices and eat the holy meat, the priestly portions, and the tithes; they are pure from all impurity and theft, like the flocks of Jacob at the time they were shorn and came up from the **Jabbok stream**, for there was no impurity or theft among them. 

He said, **"Your lips are like a thread of scarlet"** regarding the **High Priest** who would pray with his lips on **Yom Kippur** <term>Yom Kippur: the Day of Atonement, the holiest day of the year</term> and transform the sins of Israel—even if they were as red as scarlet, they would become white as snow. He said, **"Like a slice of pomegranate..."** regarding the **King**, who is as full of **commandments** <term>Mitzvah: a religious duty or good deed</term> as a pomegranate is full of seeds, aside from the officers, governors, and deputies who are close to the King; this is the meaning of **"behind your veil."** 

He said, **"Your neck is like the tower of David"** regarding the **Head of the Academy** <note>original: "Resh Metivta"</note>; like the generation of the King, through his merit the structure of the world is sustained, and through the merit of his **Torah** <term>Torah: the body of Jewish divine law and teachings</term> they were secure and victorious in their wars, as if they held all the "shields of the mighty" in their hands. He said, **"Your two breasts"** refers to the **two Messiahs** <note>In Jewish tradition, these are the Messiah son of Joseph and the Messiah son of David</note> who are destined to redeem Israel. Regarding the inner meaning of these matters and the scriptural text, one should consult the **Targum** on these verses in their proper place.

**Rabbi Abraham ibn Ezra** <note>original abbreviation: "הרחב"ע"</note> interpreted them as follows: **"Your eyes"** are the prophets and seers; **"Your hair"** refers to the Nazirites; **"Your teeth"** are the mighty warriors; **"Your lips"** are the singers; **"Your brow"** <note>or "temple"</note> refers to the priests; **"Your neck"** is the King; the "shields" are the princes of the surroundings; and the "two breasts" are the two authorities: the **Written Torah and the Oral Torah**. 

Close to this path is the way of the **Midrash**, which posits that even the "lowliest" <note>original: "pohu," likely "poshei," meaning the sinners or simplest folk</note> among them is pure, praising each and every part of the "Wall" according to their value and their task, as fits their path. For in all their parts, there is total merit to cause the union of God, may He be blessed, with Israel, so that He may dwell among them and sustain and enliven their union. Therefore, He agreed to **Solomon's** request in this matter, and the **Divine Presence** <term>Shekhinah: the indwelling presence of God in the world</term> was pleased with the work of his hands.

Furthermore, my heart has seen another way of looking at these verses regarding the nature of everyone who intends to settle their faculties. This speaks of the limbs of the body themselves; it does not speak of their physical beauty or the goodness of their material appearance, but rather the purity of their merit and their cleanliness from all sin and guilt. This includes everything connected to the limb and the activity performed through it. This is the "praise and beauty" I have seen: to cleave to the living God, as in the matter of "You shall be holy, for I am holy." 

The scripture says **"Behold, you are beautiful"** as a general rule regarding their state from the perspective of the pure soul, which hovers above like a protective covering. She is the chosen one within the body, giving strength and preparation to the physical matter to walk in the paths of uprightness and to fulfill the **Torah** and the **commandments**. Because of this, all the limbs of the body are healed and purified, even though they are material. 

He began by praising the **eyes**, for they are the "merciful ones" <note>likely meaning the primary sensory conduits</note> that serve as the cause for purifying the body; for the "eye sees and the heart covets" <note>A common Rabbinic idiom: "The eye sees, the heart desires, and the body commits the sin"</note>, and the heart is the material, and the heart is like a pomegranate...

<meta>This page continues the allegorical interpretation of the beloved’s features in the Song of Songs. Having previously discussed the "Wall" and the general beauty of the community, the author now moves to a specific anatomical allegory, focusing on the eyes (representing spiritual vision and leadership), the hair (representing the subduing of the physical ego), and the teeth (representing the purity of what one consumes and speaks).</meta>

# Song of Songs

Regarding sight, it is said that "the eye sees," and it can be damaged and darkened by looking at evil. How much more so in this case! If one’s grace is momentary and found in the strength of this world, one should remember that when the soul is divested from the body—for the soul is a "portion of wisdom"—He who gave power and vision to the eye will find it damaged, diminished, and darkened. 

The Holy One, Blessed be He, praised the wedding canopy and the grace that blossomed in her eyes, saying: **"Your eyes are doves."** <note>Song of Songs 4:1</note> This means that their eyes possess a grace without any blemish at all, resulting from the gravity of their sight; they refuse to look upon an evil thing. For the eyes are "rivers" flowing from the spirits of the brain, and they are pure and bright without any mixture or external existence. It is not even a question of the eyes being dimmed or darkened by the mundane; rather, their sight is so elevated that they bring light <note>original: "חורה" - likely a variant of "אורה" (light) or "חוורא" (whiteness/purity)</note> to the other members of the body and even to the whole world through their vitality. They focus only on matters of commandment and the study of the Torah. This is the secret of **"Your eyes are doves,"** as our Sages of blessed memory said: "Just as this dove brought light to the world..." <note>A reference to the Midrash regarding the dove from Noah's ark, or the light of the Menorah in the Temple</note>

Since hair is also a tool for the strengthening of the **Evil Inclination** <term>Evil Inclination (Yetzer HaRa): the innate human drive toward physical pleasure or selfish desires</term> when a person beautifies themselves with it—as our Sages of blessed memory said of **Joseph the Righteous**, that "he used to curl his hair..." <note>A midrashic tradition that Joseph’s focus on his appearance led to his trials with Potiphar's wife</note>—it is also mentioned in praise of one who suppresses his impulses. This refers to the story of the young man who curled his hair and came before **Simeon the Just** <term>Simeon the Just: a High Priest during the Second Temple period known for his piety</term> to explain his reason for becoming a **Nazirite** <term>Nazirite: someone who took a vow of holiness, including abstaining from wine and letting their hair grow</term>. Simeon said to him: "May such as you increase in Israel!" 

The arrangement of the hair is like a "revealed" thing that could be a cause for the strengthening of the Evil Inclination, but here it is internal, like incense that is contained. This reflects the blossoming of humility in the hair. This grace is like **"a flock of goats that streamed down from Mount Gilead,"** meaning that after they have been "shorn" and the hair removed, no impression of them remains at all. This symbolizes that the hair is no longer a tool for harm, even if it has the potential to cause damage as we mentioned.

-> **"Your teeth are like a flock of shorn ewes that have come up from the washing, all of which bear twins, and not one among them is bereaved."** <note>Song of Songs 4:2</note> <-

<margin>
It is known that vitality in the soul is like a "great bird" etc. The material delight of the **teeth** is that they are a tool. If one uses them for food—specifically forbidden foods—it is as they have elaborated in the **Book of Zohar, portion Shmini**.
</margin>

As it is written: "Anyone who eats of forbidden foods <note>original Aramaic: "מאכלני' דאסירי"</note> attaches themselves to the 'Other Side' <term>The Other Side (Sitra Achra): in Kabbalah, the realm of spiritual impurity or evil</term>, and they defile their soul and their body. A spirit of impurity rests upon them, and they show that they have no portion in the Higher Portion and do not come from His place and are not attached to Him..." 

The text continues in the explanation of the verse **"All the labor of man is for his mouth"** <note>Ecclesiastes 6:7</note>, saying: "Observe the scripture regarding the time when a person is judged in that world." It is written that the entire judgment and all that one suffers in that world and the punishments exacted from them are "for his mouth," because his mouth did not guard itself. It is written that he shuts off his soul and does not attach to the Side of Life, the "Right Side." Even the wise man's soul does not ascend from judgment forever and ever. It cannot ascend to its place because it was found attached to the "Other Side." 

Therefore, it should be clear to us how far the punishment for impure food reaches, and the blemish that follows a person because of it. Without doubt, the features of the face, which are the tools for eating, are blemished and a permanent defect is cast upon them if they are used for impure food. As the holy words in the **Zohar** state, such a person "shall not inherit." Therefore, they should be refined and clear, "like milk," without any blemish at all. When purity is at its highest, it is as **Ezekiel** said: "Never has there come into my mouth any offensive meat" <note>Ezekiel 4:14; original: "בשר פיגול"</note>, according to the way it is explained in the **Talmud**. So too for the person himself in his ethical conduct: all his days should be like a festival. Regarding these three things, they are compared to whiteness and strength, for they represent the beauty of majesty, spinning, and repair. This is the secret of **"Your teeth are like a flock of shorn ewes,"** which are of one "spinning" and one "measure" for all of them!

<summary>This page provides a Kabbalistic commentary on the eyes, hair, and teeth of the beloved in Song of Songs, interpreting them as symbols of spiritual purity, the subduing of the ego, and the strict avoidance of forbidden foods to prevent soul-defilement.</summary>
<keywords>Song of Songs, Eyes, Doves, Hair, Joseph the Righteous, Simeon the Just, Nazirite, Teeth, Zohar, Forbidden Foods, Sitra Achra, Purity, Ezekiel, Evil Inclination</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the allegorical analysis of the Song of Songs, transitioning from a general praise of the community to a detailed interpretation of specific anatomical features. The previous page discussed the "eyes as doves," and this page expands on how various classical commentators (the Targum, Midrash, and Ibn Ezra) identify these features with social, religious, and political roles within the nation of Israel, as well as a secondary philosophical interpretation regarding the physical body.</meta>

# Commentary

From the delicacies of Your goodness, as is explained—and our Sages of blessed memory <note>Hebrew: *Hazal*</note> said—first of all: **"Behold, you are beautiful,"** <note>Song of Songs 4:1</note> meaning your very essence and your powers are undoubtedly beautiful and pure. For they are the "seed blessed by the Lord," and their temperament and nature are pure, maintaining the "threefold cord" of Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob. From this perspective, there is beauty in the collective "Wall" <note>An allegory for the community of Israel</note> in that which was said: it has the power to cause her to cleave to the Blessed Name, who calls her "My darling," as we have said. This is the secret of **"Behold, you are beautiful."** There are also other reasons, as it says: **"Your eyes are doves..." "Your hair..." "Your teeth..."**

Indeed, the Midrash, the Translator <note>The Aramaic *Targum*</note>, and the rest of the commentators of blessed memory have agreed that these verses refer to the various parts of the "Wall's" powers and their emissaries, which are ranked one above the other; the scriptures spoke according to these divisions.

The opinion of the Translator <note>the *Targum*</note> of blessed memory is as follows:
*   **"Your eyes"** refers to the leaders of the congregation and the Sanhedrin <note>The supreme judicial council of ancient Israel</note>, who bring light to the world.
*   **"Your hair"** refers to the rest of the people, for they are all righteous like the sons of Jacob who gathered stones and made a mound on Mount Gilead.
*   **"Your teeth"** refers to the Priests <note>original: *Kohanim*</note> and the Levites who offer the sacrifices and eat the holy meat, the priestly portions, and the tithes; they are pure from all violence or theft, like the flocks of Jacob’s sheep when they were shorn and came up from the Yabbok stream, among which there was no violence or theft.
*   **"Your lips are like a scarlet thread"** refers to the High Priest who would pray with his lips on the Day of Atonement, turning the sins of Israel—though they be red as scarlet—to be white as snow.
*   **"Like a piece of pomegranate [is your temple]"** refers to the King, who is as full of commandments as a pomegranate is of seeds, as well as the officers, governors, and deputies who are close to the King; this is the secret of the phrase "behind your veil."
*   **"Your neck is like the tower of David"** refers to the Head of the Academy <note>original: *Resh Metivta*</note>; like the King’s generation, his merit sustains the structure of the world, and by the merit of his Torah study, they were secure and victorious in their wars as if they held all the shields of the mighty.
*   **"Your two breasts"** refers to the two Messiahs <note>The Messiah son of Joseph and the Messiah son of David</note> who are destined to redeem Israel. Regarding the inner content and what is hidden and covered within the scriptures, look for them in their proper places.

And Rabbi Abraham ibn Ezra <note>original: *Ha-RAba*</note> explained:
*   **"Your eyes"** are the prophets and seers.
*   **"Your hair"** are the Nazirites.
*   **"Your teeth"** are the mighty warriors.
*   **"Your lips"** are the singers.
*   **"Your temple"** are the Priests.
*   **"Your neck"** is the King.
*   **"The shields"** are the army officers.
*   **"Your breasts"** are the two Torahs: the Written Torah and the Oral Torah.

Close to this path is the way of the Midrash, which equates them by praising each and every part of the "Wall" according to its value and function. As we have said, this is fitting for our approach: in all their parts today, there is a merit that causes the connection of the Blessed Name with Israel, allowing Him to dwell among them and to establish and revive the Wall. Therefore, He agreed to Solomon's request in this matter, and the Divine Presence <note>original: *Shekhinah*</note> rested upon the work of his hands.

Know, my heart, that there is another path through these verses concerning the intended goal, following our method of settling the powers. This refers to the members of the body itself and not just their outward beauty or appearance. My teacher said these refer to the purity of their merit and their cleanness from every single sin—each member according to what depends upon it and the activity performed by it. This is the "praise and beauty" that allows one to cleave to the Living God, as in the matter of: "You shall be holy, for I am holy."

It says **"Behold, you are beautiful"** as a general statement regarding the aspect of the pure soul, which "wisdom brings forth a covering," <note>An obscure physiological/philosophical reference to the soul's influence on the brain or body</note> and it shines within the body, giving power and preparation to the physical matter to walk in the paths of life and to fulfill the Torah and the commandments. Through this, all the members of the body become refined and sanctified, even though they are material. It begins by praising the **"eyes,"** which are "merciful" <note>Likely "guides" or "leaders" in this context</note> in that they lead the movements of the body, for "the eye sees and the heart desires." Therefore, they are like a pomegranate...

->of transgression<-

# Song of Songs

<columns>
The body is free from every blemish, with the shield hanging upon it—much like a glaze over a potsherd <note>original: *ma'ayan al ha-cheres*; likely referring to a protective or decorative coating that gives a common vessel value</note>—providing a place for it to be sustained by its own strength. Despite this high standing, the soul humbles itself, for the reward is known to be doubled. Furthermore, even if the "dawn" of suffering and good deeds was lacking, it was undoubtedly present as it should be from the side of the women, as they are my strength in matters of the Blessed One. Now, through these triumphs, they stand in their strength and might; they are the "weapons of war" to destroy the enemy. This is the secret of the phrase **"all the shields of the mighty,"** <note>Song of Songs 4:4</note> which refers to the gifts given in abundance <note>original: *balchumor*, possibly a corruption of a term for "clay" or "abundance"</note> which are the tools used to "wash" and purify the soul, and all of them are suspended upon Him, as we have learned.

**Your two breasts are like two fawns, twins of a**
**gazelle, that graze among the lilies.**
<column-break/>
It appears that the text mentions "breasts" in this place 
as an analogy for those who pour out 
and bestow charity and kindness upon the poor. 
I have seen them with a desire that they should return to being 

"breasts" that are constantly pouring out and nursing with "milk" <note>An allegory for spiritual and physical sustenance</note>. Regarding this matter, the Holy One, Blessed be He, praised the Community of Israel <note>Hebrew: *Knesset Yisrael*</note>, for they are "merciful ones, the children of merciful ones, and performers of kindness, the children of performers of kindness" <note>A classic Talmudic description of the Jewish character</note>. To indicate the great quantity of this kindness, it says **"your two breasts,"** meaning they do not withhold with one hand, but give with both hands in great abundance. Regarding the speed of their action, without any laziness, it says **"like two fawns,"** for they are swift in their movement and their running. To indicate that the charity is done for its own sake <note>Hebrew: *Lishmah*</note>, without any mixture of improper or degrading thoughts, it says **"that graze among the lilies,"** from which a sweet fragrance wafts. So too, charity and kindness are a source of favor and a "pleasing aroma" before the Lord—not like the wicked, for whom giving is a burden or done only for their own benefit and comfort.

**Until the day breathes and the shadows flee,**
**I will go to the mountain of myrrh and to the hill of**
**frankincense.**
<column-break/>
This means to say: how, when these kindnesses 
are pure and clean, without 
the blemishes or defects of past transgressions as explained, 
I have forever established My dwelling and residence among them, 
and the Divine Presence <note>Hebrew: *Shekhinah*</note> shall rest upon the work of your hands. This is the secret of 

**"Until the day breathes,"** meaning all the time that the light of day breathes and spreads. This is a metaphor for the light of holiness and purity that is drawn down through the fulfillment of the Torah and the commandments. As long as this light is constant and the "shadows"—which are metaphors for the shadows of evening and lack, the side of impurity <note>Hebrew: *Tumah*</note>—flee and have no power to spread among you, then I will eternally dwell in the Holy Temple, and the Divine Presence will live among those below. This is the secret of **"I will go to the mountain of myrrh,"** etc. Specifically, it implies that if the light stops, the dwelling of the Divine Presence among those below will also stop; therefore, they should be careful to be constant in their righteousness, just as they were in the days of Solomon. It says **"to the mountain of myrrh,"** because in addition to the Temple being on Mount Moriah, it also suggests that when Israel purifies their future through their sufferings and good deeds, the Temple becomes a "mountain of myrrh and hill of frankincense," where the scent of the Temple service and the sacrifices rises up in favor. Its fragrance will waft afar like the scent of myrrh and frankincense—or like "frankincense" <note>Hebrew: *levonah*</note> which shares a root with "whitening" <note>Hebrew: *laban*</note>, for it makes the sins of Israel as white as snow. If not for this purity, the holiness of the House and the service within it would not avail them, as indeed happened in later times.

The author concludes this praise of the Community of Israel 
by saying that even though previously 
he mentioned only some of the "limbs" and not all, it is fitting 
<column-break/>
**to say: "You are altogether beautiful, my darling, and there is no flaw in you."** <note>Song of Songs 4:7</note>

# Commentary

<columns>
Behold, all of them are likened to the breadth of the appearance of whiteness, for they all begin—and particularly the Kingdom of Life—as fleshly gifts, like the divine emanation of *Remi* <note>original: *Sefirat R.M.Y.*; likely a kabbalistic reference to a specific configuration of divine light</note>. Israel was sanctified through this and was clarified from the darkness of the "walls," and therefore the Holy One, Blessed be He, praised them for this holiness. He said, "and none is barren among them" <note>Song of Songs 4:2</note> to symbolize the negation of any blemish, even though our essential life in the soul is like a dance, and our essential life in the body is in its infancy. The intention is the sanctification of their strength through pure and permitted food, as we have said.

The "donkey-like" and "murky" <note>Referring to base physical desires or crude speech</note>—there is no need to elaborate. The Divine Presence <note>original: *Shekhinah*</note> is hidden from the group of liars and flatterers.
<column-break/>
It is also known that the matter of speech is a grave sin, and the matter of the "four groups who do not receive the Divine Presence" <note>A famous Talmudic teaching (Sotah 42a) listing liars, flatterers, scoffers, and slanderers</note>—including those who speak slander—all depend on speech.

->**Your lips are like a scarlet thread, and your speech**<-
->**is comely; your temple is like a piece of a pomegranate**<-
->**within your locks.**<-

The punishment for "foulness of the mouth" <note>vulgarity</note> is as great as is well known. Here, He praises the Community of Israel for the absence of forbidden speech, for they are permitted and focused in their speech and careful with it. He said, **"Your lips are like a scarlet thread,"** which refers to the beauty and elegance of the lips; being like a scarlet thread indicates that they are in perfection without any blemish at all. This is a hint at their being in perfection without the blemish or defect associated with them—namely, the forbidden speech of which we spoke. 

Aside from this, they possess another perfection related to their use in the study of Torah, which is a "bride-like" and beautiful speech, through which the Name, Blessed be He, is praised and exalted. This is the meaning of **"and your speech is comely."** The intention also refers to the matter of prayer and blessing which issues from within, and in this, it is also appropriate and correct. Regarding the absence of "brazenness of face" <note>impudence or arrogance</note>, which is a great sin—as the Sage said, "The brazen-faced are headed for Gehenna" <note>Pirkei Avot 5:20</note>—He said, **"your temple is like a piece of a pomegranate."** For the "temple" <note>the side of the face/brow</note> is the source of the face, like the divine emanation of *Resi* <note>original: *Sefirat R.S.Y.*</note>. Due to its gravity, He uses the analogy of **"a piece of a pomegranate"** to indicate beauty and repair without any blemish at all. This is a hint at the absence of the "wrathful blemish" associated with them, which is the blemish of anger. He said **"within your locks"** to indicate modesty and "shamefacedness" <note>a virtuous sense of shame or humility</note>, using the image of hair which is an internal ornament of the face.

The whole is as if it were opposite, 
indicating an upright stature which is an 
honor for man. Therefore, this neck, 
which is the beauty of the body...
<column-break/>
It is known that the neck is the beauty of 
the human body, as the Sages of blessed memory mentioned 
in the Midrash. As he mentioned it in man, 
so this neck, which is the beauty...

->**Your neck is like the tower of David built for an armory,**<-
->**whereon there hang a thousand bucklers, all shields of**<-
->**mighty men.**<-

In this part of the body, it is possible for a "criminal iniquity" to be drawn forth, which is the matter of pride—walking with an upright stature. Concerning anyone who is prideful, the Sages said that the Divine Presence departs from him, and he is as one who worships idols, as brought in the Midrash. Yet here it is a praise in the matters of the Blessed One. He said of the neck that it is "grace," and it enables one to dwell in the world in a way that the vessel prepares a person for battles for the sake of Heaven, and to complete the strength and might of the person in what pertains to the soul. 

To this end, he said that in the Community of Israel, the "wall" <note>moral defense</note> follows this path: **"Your neck is like the tower of David."** Even though the neck is high and beautifies the stature and the entire body, much like the tower of David which is grand in its space, nevertheless it is **"built for an armory"** <note>original: *talpiyot*</note>. This indicates it is a "hill" <note>Hebrew: *tel*</note> toward which all "mouths" <note>Hebrew: *piyot*</note> turn <note>A classic Midrashic wordplay on *Talpiyot* as the hill (the Temple) toward which all pray</note> because of its beautiful appearance; it has no blemish or defect at all, and it does not allow the essential life to cease the strength of the body.
<meta>catchword: the body</meta>

<summary>This page provides a moral and mystical commentary on Song of Songs 4:3-4, interpreting the lips and temples as symbols of pure speech, Torah study, and modesty, while the neck represents a balance between dignity and the avoidance of pride.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the allegorical commentary on Chapter 4 of the Song of Songs. The previous page concluded the discussion of the "neck" and the "shields" (v. 4). This page moves to the "breasts" (v. 5), the "mountain of myrrh" (v. 6), and the declaration of total beauty (v. 7), interpreting these as symbols of communal charity and the sanctity of the Holy Temple.</meta>

...side of the narrow place, rather, it is in place of the shield hanging upon it. This shield protects against the poison <note>original: *Heres* or *Aras*; likely referring to the venom of the primordial serpent or the "evil inclination"</note> so that there is a place for it to be revealed. Despite all this, it makes itself secondary, for the reward is double, as is known. Also, all the reward for the strikes <note>original: *makkot*; refers to the "pains" or "tribulations" of spiritual discipline</note> and the good deeds are certainly necessary from the side of what is revealed in their vitality, though hidden in the eyes of the Blessed One. Now, from the side of the falls, they are in their strength and power, and they are weapons to destroy the poison. So too are all the "skins of the heroes," which are the strikes that are like weapons for the soul, and all of them hang upon it, as we have stated.

# Your two breasts are like two fawns, twins of a gazelle, that graze among the lilies.

<margin>
It appears that "your breasts" is used in this context as an example of "hands," which pour out and bestow charity and kindness upon the poor. They are beloved for the strength of their light...
</margin>
They are arranged such that they constantly pour out and provide milk. Regarding this matter, the Holy One, Blessed be He, praised the Community of Israel for being "compassionate children of compassionate parents, and performers of kindness—the children of those who perform kindness" <note>A classic Rabbinic idiom describing the inherent character of the Jewish people</note>. To indicate the greatness of the quantity, He said "your two breasts," suggesting that your strength is not given with just one hand, but with both hands and in abundance. Regarding the matter of speed without laziness, He said "like two fawns," for they are swift in their movement and their running. To indicate that the charity is performed for its own sake, without any mixture of improper or distracting thoughts, He said "that graze among the lilies"—a good fragrance wafts from them. So too, charity and kindness are for favor and a "pleasing aroma" <note>original: *rei'ach nichoach*; a term usually reserved for Temple sacrifices</note> before the Lord; they are not like the "kindness of the wicked," which is actually a sin <note>A reference to Proverbs 14:34, often interpreted to mean that the kindness of heathens is done for ulterior motives</note> because of your removals and your withholdings.

# Until the day breathes and the shadows flee, I will go to the mountain of myrrh and to the hill of frankincense.

<margin>
This means that when the aforementioned companions are pure and clean, without blemish or the defect of transgression, as chosen kindness, then I will dwell among you continuously. The Divine Presence will rest upon the work of your hands. This is the secret of:
</margin>
"Until the day breathes"—this means as long as the light of the day breathes and spreads. This is an allegory for the light of holiness and purity that flows from the fulfillment of the Torah and its commandments. As long as this light persists, "the shadows flee"—this is an allegory for the shadows of evening and the darkness of the "side of impurity" <note>original: *sitra achra*; the Kabbalistic term for the forces of evil</note>, which will then have no power to spread among you. For then I will cause my Divine Presence <note>original: *Shekhinah*</note> to dwell in the Holy Temple, and the Divine Presence will be among the lower realms. This is the secret of "I will go to the mountain of myrrh," etc. Specifically, if the light does not engage, the Divine Presence will cease from the lower realms; therefore, they should be careful to be stringent in their righteousness, just as they were in the days of Solomon. He said "to the mountain of myrrh," etc., for besides the fact that the Holy Temple is like Mount Moriah, he further intends that when Israel purifies their choices through their strikes and good deeds, the Holy Temple becomes a "mountain of myrrh and a hill of frankincense." Then the aroma of the service and the sacrifices will rise with favor and will waft far away like the scent of myrrh and frankincense. Alternatively, "frankincense" <note>original: *levonah*</note> relates to "whitening" <note>original: *lavan*</note> the sins of Israel like snow. Otherwise, the holiness of the House and the service within it would not avail them, as indeed happened afterward.

# You are altogether beautiful, my love, and there is no blemish in you.

<margin>
He concludes with this praise of the Community of Israel, saying that even though He mentioned some parts previously and not all of them, it should not be thought [that only those parts are perfect]...
</margin>

<summary>This page interprets the Song of Songs as an allegory for Israel’s communal virtues, viewing "breasts" as hands that give charity and "frankincense" as the purification of sins. It emphasizes that the Divine Presence dwells among the people only when they maintain moral purity and perform good deeds with alacrity.</summary>

<meta>The previous page discussed verses 5 through 7 of Chapter 4, focusing on the "breasts" as symbols of charity. This page returns to a detailed analysis of the "lips," "temples," and "neck" (verses 3-4), interpreting these physical features as allegories for pure speech, humility, and the rejection of arrogance.</meta>

# Commentary

<meta>catchword: The Body</meta>

The letter H <note>original: *he*; likely a reference to a specific section or a mystical designation of the Divine Name</note> refers to the totality. Its meaning is derived from the "breadth," a likeness of the "whiteness" <note>Symbolizing purity or mercy</note> that was within it from the beginning. Regarding the health of the tongue, there is "no blemish in his flesh" as has been explained. That is to say, Israel was sanctified through this and distinguished from the rest of the nations. Therefore, the Holy One, Blessed be He, praised them for this sanctity, saying "all of you" <note>Referring to Song of Songs 4:7, "Thou art all fair... there is no spot in thee"</note> are without any blemish. This refers to the negation of any "anointing of the face" <note>meaning external pretension or false appearance</note>, for they possess an internal quality of soul just as the body itself acts in its smaller parts. The intention here is the pursuit of holiness, even regarding food that is pure and permitted, as we have discussed.

->It is also known that the matter of speech is a<-
->great and mighty sin; there is no need to<-
->elaborate upon it. The matter of the four groups<-
->who do not receive the Divine Presence<-
->—the mockers, the liars, the hypocrites,<-
->and the slanderers—all of them depend on speech:<-

This concerns "obscenity of the mouth" <note>original: *nivlut peh*; vulgar or shameful speech</note>, the punishment for which is great and well-known. This passage praises the Assembly of Israel for avoiding forbidden speech, for they are permitted and focused in their words, and cautious of them. It says:
**"Thy lips are like a thread of scarlet"** <note>Song of Songs 4:3</note>. What is the beauty and perfection of the lips? Being like a thread of scarlet indicates that they are in their full perfection without any defect at all. This is a hint that they are perfect, without the flaw or blemish associated with them—namely, the absence of the "forbidden speech" which we have chosen to avoid. Aside from this, they possess a further perfection: they are used for the study of the Torah, which is the speech of a "bride" <note>original: *kallah*; also a play on the word for "complete" or "perfect"</note>, beautiful and through which the Blessed Name is praised and exalted. This is the meaning of **"and thy speech is comely."** If one intends this to refer to the matter of prayer—its internal purity and desirable intention—it also fits correctly. Regarding the absence of "brazenness of face," which is a great sin—as the Midrash <note>Ancient Rabbinic homiletic teachings</note> says: "The brazen-faced is headed for Gehenna" <note>Purgatory or hell</note>—the text says: **"Thy temples are like a pomegranate split open."** For your "temple" <note>original: *rakatech*</note> refers to the front of the face, as Rashi <note>Rabbi Shlomo Yitzchaki, the preeminent medieval commentator</note> of blessed memory explained. The metaphor of a **"pomegranate split open"** is to demonstrate beauty and repair without any flaw, a likeness to the absence of external flaws. It says **"behind your veil"** <note>original: *mib'ad le-tzamatech*</note> to indicate humility and "shamefacedness" <note>a virtuous modesty</note>, like the hair which covers the face from vapors.

->It is known that the neck is the beauty of the entire<-
->body, because it sits at the height,<-
->as our Sages of blessed memory said in the Midrash;<-
->it is an indication of an upright stature, which is a<-
->metaphor. He said to us that this neck, which is beauty...<-

**"Thy neck is like the tower of David builded for an armory, whereon there hang a thousand shields, all shields of mighty men."** <note>Song of Songs 4:4</note>

...in the body. It is possible for a "criminal sin" <note>original: *avon palili*</note> to extend from it, namely the matter of pride/arrogance <note>original: *ga'avah*</note> when walking with an "upright stature" <note>meaning a haughty, stiff-necked posture</note>. Anyone who is zealous regarding this is told that a needle will cause them to withdraw—as in the story of Levi bar Sisi brought in the Midrash <note>A sage who was unable to straighten his neck after a specific incident or prayer</note>. Arrogance is also loathsome in the eyes of the Blessed One, who said of the arrogant man: "I and he cannot dwell together in the world." Using "pure language" <note>refined speech</note> without understanding the "neck" is used to increase wisdom and to heal the strength and power of the neck as it pertains to the Assembly. For this reason, it says that with the Assembly of Israel, it is not so; rather, **"Thy neck is like the tower of David."** Even though the neck is high and gives prominence to the stature and the whole body—like the tower of David which is great and many-sided—nevertheless, it is **"built for an armory"** <note>original: *talpiyot*; traditionally interpreted by the Sages as *tel piyot*—the hill (tel) toward which all mouths (piyot) turn in prayer</note>. This indicates that it is a "hill" toward which everyone turns for its beautiful appearance; it has no blemish or defect at all. 

<summary>This page provides an allegorical interpretation of the lover's features in Song of Songs, equating the lips and temples with pure speech and humility, while the "neck like the tower of David" represents strength and prayer without the sin of arrogance.</summary>

<meta>The previous page concluded a discussion on the purity of speech and the physical metaphors of the "lips" and "temples" in Song of Songs, emphasizing how Israel was distinguished from other nations through their sanctified speech and rejection of "obscenity of the mouth." This page shifts to the historical and spiritual state of Israel at the time of the Exodus, interpreting their labor in Egypt as an allegory for material entrapment and explaining their redemption through Divine love and ancestral merit.</meta>

# Song of Songs

"...doing so until their forgiveness, occupied with mud and bricks; I was small, and I inherited you," etc. <note>original: *asoti ken... b'tit u-levanim*; likely referencing a Midrash regarding Israel's low state in Egypt.</note>

The plain meaning of these words is clear: they specify the details of construction and the building of bricks. The intention is that even though they were degraded and soiled from the labor of mud and bricks, He [God] brought them close to Himself. This occurred even though they were inferior and at a very low level, such that it was not seemingly in keeping with His glory, blessed be He, to involve Himself with them and redeem them while they were a despised and downtrodden people. They were not like other nations who have time to preen and adorn themselves, for one cannot approach the Supreme Being in a degraded garment, but only with cleanliness, adornment, perfumes, and "women's cosmetics." <note>A reference to Esther 2:12, used here as a metaphor for spiritual preparation.</note>

The "depth of the matter" <note>term: *omak shel devarim*; the deeper allegorical or mystical layer of the text</note> is that their occupation with "mud and bricks" is a hint toward physical and material concerns and those actions that cloud the soul. They were immersed in the material "idols of Egypt." It would have been appropriate for them to be given a long period of time before drawing near, to "gather their palms" <note>an idiom for refining one's actions</note> with the soul's adornments, consisting of good deeds and the purity from every evil thing, so that they would be worthy of approaching and cleaving to Him, blessed be He. Yet, out of the greatness of His love, He did not look at their lack of good deeds. Despite all the thickness and coarseness of the materiality that was within them, He "skipped" <note>referring to the 'leaping' over the end of time or the 'skipping' over the mountains in Song of Songs 2:8</note> and redeemed them.

Based on the earlier explanation of our Sages, we can explain according to our way: the Holy One, blessed be He, says to the Assembly of Israel regarding the redemption, "I have cherished the closeness of being near you, in that I brought you close to Me to be with Me, our King, even from the midst of mud and bricks, as we explained." Nevertheless, there is also great praise for you in this: that you agreed to come with Me—"with Me, our King"—meaning that despite being sunk in mud and matter, and being very far from the perfection of deeds, and having almost no recognition or providence regarding the knowledge of My service—knowing neither good nor evil—despite all this, you chose to come to Him and to Me. And I, of My own will, chose to show you gratitude for this, as the verse says: "I remember for thee the affection of thy youth, the love of thine espousals..." <note>Jeremiah 2:2</note>

Thus, there is merit here in the soul cleaving to You, and His wisdom prepared this in the lower worlds. Furthermore, there is the "merit of the ancestors" <note>term: *zechut avot*; the spiritual credit inherited from Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob</note> and the matter of receiving the Torah. The earlier nations did not want to receive it, but Israel's merit was in their acceptance of it. These are the arguments Solomon used earlier regarding the verse, "Who is this that cometh up from the wilderness... perfumed with myrrh..." <note>Song of Songs 3:6</note> as explained in its place. 

This is the meaning of "Depart from the peak of Amana" <note>Song of Songs 4:8; original: *tashuri mi-rosh amana*</note>. This means: you have already provided a gift and an offering from long ago, which makes you worthy of My presence. The matter is further clarified: "From the peak of Senir and Hermon..." <note>Song of Songs 4:8</note>. As explained in the Zohar, portion *Shemot* <note>Exodus</note>: 
> "Do not depart from the peak of Amana" <note>original Aramaic: *al tashuri mi-rosh amana*</note>—from the beginning of the words, from the paths of the masters of faith. These "words" refer to Jacob. And the reference to "Senir and Hermon" refers to those who were destined to receive My Torah at Mount Sinai and Hermon, and to offer their prayers upon them. "From the lions' dens"—these are the idolatrous nations who are compared to lions and leopards who cause them suffering, etc.

By their words, the Holy One, blessed be He, meant: "Depart from the peak of Amana"—this is the merit of the ancestors. "From the peak of Senir and Hermon"—this is the receiving of the Torah. This merit is strengthened even more because they come from "lions' dens," etc. This is what the verse means: "The Lord came from Sinai, and rose up from Seir unto them; he shined forth from mount Paran..." <note>Deuteronomy 33:2</note>. Against these, he said "all of them from the lions' dens, from the mountains of the leopards." These words and praises for the Assembly of Israel strengthen Solomon's arguments above against the angels, as we have said.

Or perhaps he means that until now, in all his words and praises, he praises the Assembly of Israel in all the parts of the "friends" <note>the members of the body or the community</note> in general and in detail. The main intention was to say that through the receiving of the Divine Torah and its ways—which are "ways of pleasantness"—and its uniqueness to the people of God, all the parts of the members were prepared...

It further explains that Israel's worthiness stems from the merit of the Patriarchs and their willingness to accept the Torah, unlike other nations.</summary>

# Commentary

From this, regarding the matters that depend on the future, the companions <note>term: *haverim*; literally "friends" or "companions," often referring to a circle of Torah scholars or the collective of Israel as God’s companions.</note> were sinning in them, and thus tomorrow the companions were found to be flawed. For if so, there would be a great hindrance to you in preventing the descent of the Divine Presence <note>term: *Shekhinah*; the indwelling presence of God in the world.</note> among the lower beings, Heaven forbid. As it was taught and established, [God] did not hesitate to mention only the "distant companions," <note>Referring to those far from holiness due to sin.</note> and he speaks of the "tomorrow" of the companions. And the truth <note>The Hebrew text here uses "חמת" which is a variation or OCR error for "אמת" (truth).</note> is that your beauty is seen within the collective of all the companions; even if there were some blemish or defect of transgressions among them, you are still entirely beautiful, my beloved. This is due to the lack of the performance of the commandments <note>term: *mitzvot*; religious duties or good deeds.</note> and the presence of sin. This is based on the verse "Until the day breathes" <note>Song of Songs 4:6</note> because the matter of the Divine Presence being in the Holy Temple—as it says, "I will go to the mountain of myrrh," etc.—helps to remove all things that bring about a blemish through the act of the sacrifices, which atone and purify, leaving them clean and pure.

<columns>2</columns>
**With me from Lebanon, my bride, with me from Lebanon**
**You shall come; look from the top of Amana,**
**From the top of Senir and Hermon, from the dens of**
**lions, from the mountains of leopards.**
<column-break/>
After He has completed the praise of the Assembly of Israel <note>term: *Knesset Yisrael*; the personification of the Jewish people.</note> in every detail of her members—the "near ones" who are perfect without any defect or blemish at all, as was explained—and also collectively, as we said, "You are all beautiful, my beloved," etc., He now states the purpose of His speech. This concludes the decree against the "accusing angels," <note>original: *hamalachim hamonim ma'ala*; the heavenly hosts who question why God would dwell among flawed humans.</note> the heavenly multitudes who are jealous regarding the fact that the Divine Presence dwells among the lower beings. He says that from now on, we should expect the "whitening" <note>note: a pun on *Levanon* (Lebanon) and *libbun* (to whiten or purify).</note> of the companions and the refinement of the body, about which I said that you are worthy of being with Me, attached to Me and in My company. For I am with you, like a groom with his bride in "Lebanon." This is the meaning of "With me from Lebanon, my bride." He explains "Lebanon" as the process of whitening. He says, "With me from Lebanon you shall come," meaning: Behold, since your body and your companions have become whole through the fulfillment of the commandments, and without blemish or flaw, cleansed from sins and guilt, you are worthy of unification with Me in closeness and attachment. Because I am also with you, in addition to this, I have dwelled among you and My Presence is within you. This is a great cause for the persistence and strengthening of the attachment in a strong bond. For without a doubt, when the Divine Presence dwells in the Temple, a spirit of purity and holiness is drawn from it upon all of Israel. Then there is no place for the spirit of impurity to rest upon them easily, and they are found to be far from wickedness. Furthermore, if a mishap of sin or iniquity should occur, they are repaired by means of the sacrifices. Thus, the Holy Temple, which is called "Lebanon," is the factor that strengthens the attachment and brings them closer. This is "With me from Lebanon you shall come." Furthermore, from another side, you are worthy to behold the pleasantness of the Lord and to visit His Sanctuary. This is through the merit of the early ones, and the merit of the righteous and pious ones who exist in every single generation. This is "Look from the top of Amana," etc., just as our Sages of blessed memory expounded in the Midrash: "From the top of Amana"—this is Abraham, about whom it is written, "and he believed (*he'emin*) in the Lord"; "From the top of Senir"—this is Isaac, etc.; "And Hermon"—this is Jacob, etc. Therefore, the text concludes with "From the dens of lions and the mountains of leopards," referring to those who are mighty in Torah, the pious ones, and the men of action who existed in the early generations, whose merit is worthy to stand for Israel so that the Divine Presence may dwell among them, joined to the merit of the Patriarchs as mentioned. And in the Book of the Zohar, Parashat Shemot, <note>Zohar, Exodus section.</note> it says: "Rabbi Yudan says: 'From the dens of lions'—these are the Torah scholars who occupy themselves with the Torah in the dwellings of the study halls and in the synagogues, for they are the lions and leopards of the Torah."

->Or it may be interpreted<-

"With me from Lebanon, my bride": They said in the Midrash <note>Midrash Rabbah on Song of Songs.</note> that we have learned that a marriage contract (*ketubah*) is written for a virgin... [whereby] a man commits himself to provide for her... and I to her...

It explains that the indwelling of the Divine Presence is made possible through the merit of the Patriarchs and the dedication of Torah scholars.</summary>

# Song of Songs

And also "with one <gloss>with one</gloss> of your eyes," for the "eyes" are a reference to the Sanhedrin <note>The high court of seventy-one sages in ancient Jerusalem who served as the supreme religious and legal authority.</note>, who are the "eyes of the community," just as our Sages of blessed memory interpreted the verse "your eyes are like doves." It says "with one <gloss>with one</gloss> of your eyes" because it is known that there were many courts in Israel and also in the Temple courtyards, as is known. He explains that regarding those [courts] here in the "ladder" <note>A metaphorical reference to the hierarchy of holiness or the levels of the soul.</note>, he spoke of the side of the Chamber of Hewn Stone <note>term: *Lishkat HaGazit*; the specific hall in the Temple where the Great Sanhedrin sat.</note>, which is one <gloss>unique</gloss> and the most unique of them all, being the primary one. The labor [of study] washes them, and afterward, he has the affection of the people. Selecting from their merit is preferable for preparing the heart of God toward goodness. 

And also "with one jewel of your necklace": our Sages of blessed memory explained this regarding the specific details and the services of bringing [offerings] into the Temple, which are like ornaments and necklaces. He crowned them to give us pleasure through all of them. This is the inner meaning of "with one jewel of your necklace." The Holy Temple is explained as being like "ice," as written in the *Pardes* <note>A classic work of Kabbalah by Rabbi Moshe Cordovero.</note> regarding the verse "your neck is like a tower of David." He said "your necklaces" in the plural to refer to all the parts of the Temple: the courtyards, the chambers, the Sanctuary, the Hall, and the Holy of Holies. 

Alternatively, "with one jewel of your necklace" refers to the "Radiance" <note>term: *Havraka*; a mystical flash of divine light.</note> which sustains the worlds and brings down abundance to all of them, the upper and lower. He spoke of them in the manner of "the giver" and "the receiver," which is the power of the concept of Blessing. Here we find Justice and Righteousness, which are the foundations of the world, and in their merit, Israel is redeemed, as it is written: "Zion shall be redeemed with justice, and those who return to her with righteousness." In this sense, "necklace" (*anak*) is from the language of "You shall surely provide <gloss>*ha’anek ta’anikenu*</gloss> for him" <note>Deuteronomy 15:14; referring to the gifts given to a departing servant.</note>. And "your necklaces" refers to the unique noble people of Israel in their kindness and charity, who are like the splendors of the wall, high in stature and beauty in the eyes of the nations. 

The Midrash has already explained something close to this: "with one jewel of your necklace"—this refers to Moses, the unique and mighty one among your tribes. 

Or, he may mean "You have captured my heart" <note>Song of Songs 4:9</note> etc., regarding the concept of the Unity <note>term: *Yichud*; the central Jewish act of declaring God's absolute oneness.</note> that Israel establishes, stating that the Lord is God. For they unify the Name of God every day, and it is known that this matter causes all the goodness of the worlds. By accepting this Unity, the upper forms are unified, the worlds are linked, and the abundance and goodness descend and increase in all of them. Since the matter of Unity begins in the heart, as written in the Zohar <note>The foundational work of Jewish mysticism.</note> on the portion *Vayechi*, our Sages of blessed memory said: Rabbi Eleazar opened and said: "'You shall know this day and return it to your heart that the Lord is God' <note>Deuteronomy 4:39</note>... This verse should have read 'You shall know this day that the Lord is God AND return it to your heart.' Rather, he meant to say: if you wish to stand upon this and to know that the Lord is God, return it to your heart, and then your heart will know that they are one and as one, included one in the other, and they become one. Then you will find that the Lord is God, for the Merciful One and the Law <note>Aramaic original: *Rachmana ve-Iqa*; representing the divine attributes of Mercy and Judgment.</note> are included as one, and concerning this: 'return it to your heart' to know the matter." 

It is clearly found in the words of Rabbi Eleazar that the matter of Unity is established and sealed from the heart, as stated. Therefore, see how great is the power of Unity in the heart! This is the secret of "You have captured my heart, my sister, my bride"—meaning, <gloss>with one</gloss> you have made My laws like the hearts which are one, as mentioned; so it is through the "one" unique Unity every single day. 

And he said that the Unity is performed through one of two aspects. First, through the study of Torah with intention—moving from the Written Torah to the Oral Torah, and from the Oral Torah to the Tractates—and through prayer, which is the "service of the heart." All of this is of one kind, depending on speech, and concerning this, he said "You have captured my heart, my sister, my bride" through the speech of the verse. 

Afterward, he mentions the second part of the essence of Unity: it is through the performance of the commandments <note>term: *Mitzvot*.</note>. For all of them are gathered for this intention, as is known to those who "know grace" <note>A common euphemism for students of Kabbalah.</note>. This part is divided into three aspects corresponding to the removal and subduing of material concerns for the fulfillment of the commandments. This is the secret of "with one of your eyes," meaning with one of your actions, which are patterned after the eye. For the eye leads the person to walk from place to place, and it also...

It emphasizes that the Unity of God is achieved through both the speech of Torah/prayer and the physical performance of commandments.</summary>
