<meta>The previous page discussed the Sanhedrin as the "eyes" of the community and the unique sanctity of the Chamber of Hewn Stone. This page continues the mystical interpretation of Song of Songs 4:8-9, transitioning from the physical structure of the Holy Temple to the human body and the collective of Israel as a "chariot" for the Divine Presence.</meta>

# Commentary

It is necessary to accept a great sovereignty, for the Torah was inclusive of all matters pertaining to the perfection of every single member [of the community], whether in "turning away from evil" or "doing good," as has been chosen. After establishing the truth of this matter, he says that one must reach the ultimate fulfillment: to bring [the soul] from potentiality into action, to elevate the companions from the coarseness and thickness of their material nature, and to refine and purify them through the Torah and the commandments. And so that this task does not seem too difficult for them—since a great effort is required for a person to subdue their "evil inclination" <note>The *Yetzer Hara*, or innate selfish/base impulse in Jewish thought.</note> which stands at their right hand to accuse them—he says that they will receive assistance from Heaven. As our Sages said: "One who comes to purify himself is assisted" <note>A famous Talmudic maxim from Tractate Yoma 38b.</note>. This is the meaning of "With me from Lebanon, my bride" (Song of Songs 4:8). That is to say, the most precious part that is distilled and ascends "from Lebanon"—meaning from his body and his heart <note>A wordplay on *Levanon* (Lebanon) and *Lev* (heart).</note>, as we mentioned in the Midrash and explained above—is "with me," through my assistance and my help. 

He says further: Know and see, for your preparation is not merely to merit that my Divine Presence <note>term: *Shekhinah*; the indwelling presence of God in the world.</note> should dwell in the Temple alone. Rather, we shall also purify your bodies and your very selves so that the Divine Presence may dwell within you literally, and you shall become a Chariot <note>term: *Merkavah*; a mystical concept where righteous individuals become a vehicle for Divine manifestation.</note> for the Divine Presence. This was a great achievement: the person makes a "Holy Temple" out of their own body and their companions. This is the secret of "With me from Lebanon you shall come"—this "building" refers to the Holy Temple. And the secret of "if you remain" <note>original: *im tityaru*</note> means: "With me, in my company, you shall come," and you will have a cleaving <note>term: *devekut*; mystical communion or attachment to God.</note> to me even greater than the cleaving that follows from the "Lebanon" which is the [physical] Holy Temple; it is an essential cleaving, like the human body itself.

And if this achievement of cleaving seems [distant] in your eyes, leave the vanities of the world. "From the top of Amana look" (Song of Songs 4:8): look at all the heads of the holy nation, the high mountains—the "lions and leopards" of the Torah in every generation. The Holy Temple was not built in their time, yet in its place, they were a Chariot for the Divine Presence. Through the power of the blessing and the abundance [of Spirit], that abundance is distributed to the whole world, so that you do not stray far from the camp. The distance of "Senir and Hermon" (Song of Songs 4:8) refers to the three Patriarchs <note>Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob.</note>, as we said in the Midrash and wrote above. "From the dens of lions," etc., refers to the heights of her people, the giants in Torah and commandments who exist in every generation, as we said previously.

-> In the name of the *Zohar* <note>The foundational work of Jewish mysticism (Kabbalah).</note>: <-

> ## **You have heartened me, my sister, my bride, you have heartened me**
> ## **With one of your eyes, with one jewel**
> ## **Of your necklace.**

For the sustaining and strengthening of the dwelling of the Divine Presence among the lower realms: the power of your achievement from the lower realms provides a great benefit that does not exist coming from the higher realms. This is the cause of the "rectification of the Mind" <note>The Kabbalistic idea of *Tikun*, or restoration/mending.</note>, which gives strength to the "Mighty One on High" through your good deeds. And if it is from the power of a great abundance and an extra blessing in all the worlds together, it is regarding the matter of "He who rides the heavens for your help" (Deuteronomy 33:26) and the matter of "You neglected the Rock that begot you" <note>original: *Tzur yeladcha tashi* (Deuteronomy 32:18); interpreted here to mean that human neglect "weakens" the Divine capacity to provide abundance.</note>. 

Since the awakening of goodness and abundance is dependent on those below—as our Sages of blessed memory said, they are "children of doubt" <note>referring to humanity's free will</note>—their cleaving with the Name [God] ensures that the dwelling and residence of the Divine Presence will be within them. This is the secret of "You have heartened me, my sister, my bride"—from the time you accepted the Torah and entered the "yoke of the Torah and commandments," you entered to be "my sister, my bride," by giving me strength and fortitude in my heart. You caused me to have a strong and courageous heart to act with kindness with my heart, to trade in kindness and to do good for the created beings. This was the ultimate purpose of the intention of Creation. And even now, with the building of the Holy Temple, this matter is strengthened—to fortify my heart regarding this, according to the command of the individual parts of the soul upon its collective whole. He also mentioned the matter of the Sanhedrin, who are those who uphold justice, which is the preservation of the world, as it is written: "A king by justice establishes the land" (Proverbs 29:4).

<summary>This page provides a mystical commentary on Song of Songs 4:8-9, explaining that the ultimate goal of the Torah is to transform the human body and the community into a "Chariot" for the Divine Presence, rivaling or exceeding the sanctity of the physical Temple. It emphasizes the concept of theurgy—the idea that human deeds below "strengthen" the Divine capacity to bestow abundance upon the world.</summary>

<meta>The previous page established the human body and the community of Israel as a "chariot" or dwelling place for the Divine Presence (Shekhinah), emphasizing that spiritual elevation requires refining one's physical nature through the Torah and commandments with heavenly assistance.</meta>

# Song of Songs

The commandments, for without doubt they possess a beauty visible to the eye, are beneficial for the good of this world <note>Hebrew: *Olam HaZeh*.</note> to bestow an abundance of satiety and blessing upon all living things. This is a primary principle, and therefore they called it the "beauty of wisdom." **How much better is your love than wine!** (Song of Songs 4:10). One merits saying that my own essence and core are "better than wine," for wine represents the continuation of revealed utility and material matters of this world. The term "wine" is a metaphor for these things, and the intention refers to the spiritual ascent caused by them into all the upper worlds, as they exist in a state of aromatic vapor, unity, and a firm order through the performance of the commandments and the study of the Torah. 

**The scent of your oils, etc.** (Song of Songs 4:10). For the Torah is called "oil," as mentioned in the Midrash. And the phrase **How much better is your love, etc.** refers to the good that flows to those who fulfill the commandments in the World to Come <note>Hebrew: *Olam HaBa*.</note>—meaning the spiritual sweetness and delights reserved for them, which constitutes the primary good. **And the scent of your oils** refers to the "rivers of persimmon" <note>A traditional Jewish metaphor for the fragrant, spiritual rewards awaiting the righteous in the afterlife.</note> prepared for them as a reward for their life of Torah and good deeds, each according to their actions. The entire intention here is to praise and distinguish the law of the actions of the lower beings, for it is they who cause all the greatness that also becomes the portion of the angels...

-> Behold, here is explained in detail the merit of <-
-> the lower beings who lack all abundance <-

### Your lips drip honey, my bride;
### honey and milk are under your tongue; and the scent
### of your garments is like the scent of Lebanon.

-> That which descends from above and all the details of its portion <-
-> The good and the nourishment that flows to all worlds <-
-> and according to the upper angels, it is in their portion <-

The intention is to strengthen the idea that Israel is more worthy of drawing the Divine Presence <note>term: *Shekhinah*; the indwelling presence of God.</note> among them than the upper beings. To understand these matters clearly, we must first introduce what is written in the holy Zohar, in the section on *Beshalach*: 

"Every single spirit drips dew from the Holy Ancient One <note>term: *Atika Kadisha*; the most primal, hidden aspect of the Divine in Kabbalah.</note> to the Lesser Countenance <note>term: *Zeir Anpin*; the emotive attributes of the Divine.</note>, and all the holy apple fields <note>A Kabbalistic metaphor for the Garden of Eden or the collective of holy souls.</note> are blessed. From that dew which flows to those below, holy angels are nourished, each one according to its actions. Is this not the 'bread of the mighty' for all living things? For that was the nourishment that Israel ate in the wilderness." 

Rabbi [Simeon] said: "How many people are nourished at this time, receiving food and vision freely, like the creatures who look into the celestial spheres..." He further said: "From that very same nourishment, they do not eat in the exact same way..." And it is further stated there: "Come and see, all the food in the world comes from above. That food which descended from heaven like seed [the Manna] is the food of the whole world; it is a pleasant and sweet food. But the food that descends from even higher above is a food that descends in spirit and is refined through investigation; it is a more subtle food. This was given to Israel at that time from the upper side... it is a more subtle food that enters the soul more than the body, and it was called 'miserable bread' <note>referring to the Manna in Numbers 21:5, which the Israelites complained about because it was so spiritual it didn't feel 'filling' like heavy physical food.</note>. The highest food of all is the food of the 'companions' <note>The *Chavrayya*; the circle of mystical scholars.</note>, those who gaze into the depths of wisdom, who eat the food of the spirit and the soul and do not eat the food of the body at all. This comes from the highest side... for this reason, the bodies of the companions are weaker than other people, for they do not eat the food of the body at all." 

The general principle arising from what we have written—and from what is written further on in the Zohar, section *Naso*—is that there are types of food that descend and flow from above even in this time. One kindness is subtler than another. The first type of food is for the general population... while the second is thin and subtle, coming from a place of Judgment <note>Hebrew: *Din*.</note>... and from there the upper angels are sustained... and there is the food of the outer forces <note>term: *Chitzonim*; the "external" or "impure" spiritual realms.</note> as it is written in the section on *Vayishlach*...

<sig>Z.K.</sig>

<summary>This page explores the mystical nature of spiritual nourishment, comparing the material benefits of commandments in this world to the "subtle food" (Manna/Wisdom) enjoyed by angels and mystics. Using the Zohar, it argues that Israel's ability to draw down the Divine Presence through Torah study and the commandments exceeds even that of the angels.</summary>

<meta>The previous page discussed the human body as a vehicle for the Divine Presence and the beauty of the commandments in "this world" (Olam HaZeh). This page continues the commentary on Song of Songs Chapter 4, specifically focusing on the superiority of human service over that of the angels.</meta>

### Commentary 3
<meta>Catchword: and the commandments</meta>

...to guide you to be cautious and saved from harms, and to draw near to the things necessary for one's inheritance and benefit. Likewise, for one who guides and directs those walking on straight paths—distancing them from the ways of the wicked and the sinners—his actions are attributed to the "eye" <note>The "eye" here likely refers to spiritual perception or divine oversight.</note>. He mentioned this first because it encompasses the first section regarding speech. He also arranged the ethics and the guidance of others within it, placing it before himself and his companions. Regarding one’s own good deeds, he said in the section **a necklace for your neck** (Song of Songs 4:9); without a doubt, good deeds are significant ornaments and jewelry for a person. For this reason, how much "grace" is added by the necklace of the neck—they are the most important ornaments and are placed in the most prominent location.

<columns>2</columns>

It means here that when the soul finds grace, the perfect ones among them acquire great beauty and perfection by being **my sister, my bride**.

<column-break/>

## How beautiful is your love, my sister, my bride! How
## much better is your love than wine, and the scent of your oils than all
## spices!

The intention is that when you have an attachment to Me and I to you, I dwell within you like a groom with his bride, for this is a great aid in beautifying their deeds. The judgment regarding the angels and the celestial beings is very much derived from this. Through this, I have achieved another level of insight by bringing you to this greatness: namely, that the perfection of **your love**—meaning the beloved and perfect ones among you—is very precious. Their service rises above the level of the angels with greater excellence and "voice." This is because the angels do not possess <term>free choice</term> and will; they do not have an <term>Evil Inclination</term> <note>Hebrew: *Yetzer HaRa*.</note>. Consequently, they deserve no special credit for always serving their Creator. However, the "beloved ones" (the righteous humans) who choose to be loving and affectionate toward Me despite the "disturbing waters" and the dangers of this world prepared before them—who conquer their inclination and draw near to Me—there is no doubt that their level is great. This is the meaning of **How much better is your love than wine**, referring to the "pure and aged wine" <note>A metaphor for spiritual rewards or deep wisdom.</note>. As the Sages of blessed memory interpreted the verse "He brought me to the banquet house [literally, house of wine]," to show affection for the beloved ones—who are the perfect and great ones—they possess this goodness and this high degree. For this reason, it also applies generally to the commandments and good deeds. He said that in all your collectivity, you are very good for the aforementioned reason. 

Furthermore, regarding **the scent of your oils**, he used "oil" as a metaphor for good deeds. He said their "scent" is cast afar; your fragrance is so good and so pleasant, more than **all spices**. He said this regarding this world, using "spices" as a metaphor for physical and material ailments <note>The author contrasts the natural scent of spices with oil that must be "pressed," symbolizing the human struggle against material suffering.</note> which lack the sensitivity they should have. This is the same reason for the interpretation of the verse mentioning **wine**, as I wrote. And this is according to the phrase **than all spices**, where the superiority aligns with our words. It also means: how much better is the scent of your oils and your good deeds than all the spices and scents of the service of the celestial beings, their testing, and their praise. Regarding this latter reason, he nicknamed the service of the celestial beings "spices" and the service of the lower beings (humans) "oil." This is because oil does not have its good scent naturally, but rather through compounding, grinding, and pressing until it absorbs a good scent. Spices, however, give their scent through their nature and essence. Similarly, the lower beings do not have good deeds in their natural state, as most are more inclined toward evil actions; rather, it is through the "grinding" and "pressing" of the Evil Inclination that they produce a good scent. However, the celestial beings—their composition and nature are oriented toward the service of their Creator, and thus he nicknamed the varieties of their service "spices." It is also possible to interpret **your love** <note>Hebrew: *dodecha*.</note> as matters of friendship and understanding. The intention is: how great is the beauty of my deeds and your friendship and your love for Me through the fulfillment of the Torah and the commandments.

<summary>This page provides a philosophical commentary on Song of Songs 4:10, arguing that human devotion is superior to that of angels because humans must overcome their Evil Inclination through effort (symbolized by pressed oil), whereas angels serve God by nature (symbolized by spices).</summary>
<keywords>Song of Songs, free will, Evil Inclination, angels vs. humans, good deeds, Torah, fragrance metaphor, spiritual perfection</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the allegorical commentary on Chapter 4 of the Song of Songs. The previous page discussed the soul's perfection through good deeds and its relationship with the Divine. This page transitions into the power of speech, prayer, and the various levels of Torah study—ranging from the literal meaning to the hidden mystical secrets.</meta>

# Song of Songs

And afterwards, **your lips drop honeycomb** <note>original Hebrew: *Nofet titponah sefatotayich* (Song of Songs 4:11)</note>, meaning that by opening your lips in words of prayer and supplication every day, they drip down "honeycomb"—the droplets and nourishment for the world. Following this, regarding the universal nourishment, he said **honey and milk**. Just as the honeycomb is not entirely pure honey—as it contains a mixture of waste, and just as there is a part of the honeycomb that is not edible—so too in universal nourishment there is a "permitted" part that is not strictly necessary for the soul. 

Regarding the other types of nourishment which are more refined, **honey and milk**, which are contained without any mixture or trial at all, they are **under your tongue**. This means they are settled and ready in the hand of the human tongue like clay. The terms **honey and milk** indicate things bound below in this world, and also that which descends to the angels above for their nourishment and existence; everything depends on the hand of those below through physical matter. Thus, everything is seen through the perspective of the lower beings. "Honey" descends to the kings, and its scent is "milk," for milk is more refined than honey. 

He said that although its sweetness is "other" <note>likely referring to spiritual sweetness vs. physical sweetness</note>, it was fitting to bring it out from your midst, and this is due to the "clothing of matter" <note>The physical body</note> which is within you yourself and in all matters of this world. Nevertheless, this does not happen without a cause or delay, for **the scent of your garments** <note>Hebrew: *Salmotecha*</note> means the garments of matter that are upon you to house [the soul], for you hide those perfections. These "garments" return the physical matter to a spiritual and refined form.

<insert>Alternatively, he intended "Your lips drop honeycomb,"</insert> etc., to praise the Assembly of Israel <note>Hebrew: *Knesset Yisrael*, a personification of the Jewish people</note> for the strength and "taste" she causes above through the occupation of Torah and its study. This reaches the Blessed Name Himself, as it were. As it is said in the Book of the Zohar <note>The foundational work of Jewish mysticism (Kabbalah)</note> in the portion of *Beshalach*: "Anyone who strives in the Torah is alive above and alive below; the Holy One, Blessed be He, comes to [listen to] his words," etc. Behold, He, may He be blessed, listens to the voice of the one occupied with Torah and delights in his words. 

Regarding this, he said **your lips drop honeycomb**, etc., for the words of Torah are sweet and pleasant to Him, may He be blessed, and He involves Himself in them. And **honey and milk are under your tongue** refers to a much more fundamental delight: namely, the study of Torah from midnight onwards. At that time, the Holy One, Blessed be He, enters the Garden [of Eden] to delight with the righteous, and He and they listen to the voice of the one studying Torah at that hour, as it is written: "Companions listen for your voice; let me hear it" <note>Song of Songs 8:13</note>. This is as found in the aforementioned chapter in the Zohar and in many places: how great is the merit of the one who rises at midnight to occupy himself with Torah, as great delight is sweetened by him for the Holy One, Blessed be He, and for all who delight in His company in matter. Regarding this, he said **honey and milk are under your tongue**, which is a taste more pleasant than the taste of honey alone. 

He also said that although the "internal" <note>mystical</note> words of the Torah may not be present—for he is only involved in the "revealed" and the "literal" <note>Hebrew: *Pshat*, the plain or surface meaning of the text</note> which are the "garments" of the Torah—and one might think they lack the power to reach the level of the aforementioned degree of the Blessed One Himself, he therefore said: **and the scent of your garments**. This is to say that even the "revealed" words and the "literal" sense of the Torah, which are garments for the "inner" Torah, their scent rises above and the Blessed Name delights and involves Himself in them.

<insert>Or he might mean:</insert> Even though your distance [from Me] is known, since you are great in rank and from a distant height, His dwelling remains with you more than with all kings. Let it not enter your mind because of this to neglect the matters of the secrets of the Torah and its inner workings from your thoughts, so as not to occupy yourself with them in depth or reveal them to every passerby. Unlike kings who occupy themselves with the Torah in its inner "garment" without any fear at all, be careful of this—do not do so. Rather, **your lips drop honeycomb, O bride**, meaning words of Torah likened to honeycomb and droplets; influence others with these. But those that are sweet as **honey and milk**, which are the [hidden] matters...

<meta>catchword: holy things</meta>

<summary>This page provides a mystical commentary on Song of Songs 4:11, explaining how prayer and Torah study—particularly at midnight—provide spiritual nourishment to the world and delight God. It distinguishes between the "revealed" (literal) and "hidden" (mystical) layers of Torah, comparing them to honeycomb, honey, and milk.</summary>

--- Page 72 ---
<meta>This page continues the mystical exploration of Song of Songs, specifically focusing on the spiritual nature of nourishment and the different levels of Divine sustenance that descend into the world. It builds on the previous page's discussion of "honey and milk" as metaphors for refined spiritual flow.</meta>

### Commentary

This was the food that descended for Israel in the desert, as it is written, **bread of heaven** <note>original: *lechem ha-shamayim* (Exodus 16:4)</note>. However, this fourth level is a nourishment that is even more elevated, holy, thin, refined, spiritual, and soulful. It is the food of the **Companions** <note>original: *chavrayya*—the circle of mystical scholars around Rabbi Shimon bar Yochai</note> and the wise who occupy themselves with the Torah constantly. This food is drawn from the place called **Wisdom** <note>term: *Chokhmah*—the second of the ten Sefirot (Divine emanations), representing the flash of intuition and the root of intellect</note>. They merit this because, through their engagement with the Torah—which is drawn from its source in that place—they ascend and enter the very root of the Torah's essence. From there, they draw down an elevated and refined nourishment. Its primary effect is on the soul and the spirit, strengthening it for the service of its God. For this reason, their bodies are often weak, because this is not food for the body, but only for its maintenance and survival; it is not meant to provide physical power, heroism, or bodily strength.

Indeed, it might appear at first glance that there is a contradiction in these matters. From the beginning of the discussion, it seems that the food of the **Companions** is the same food that Israel ate in the desert, for it says "man ate the bread of the mighty" <note>original: *lechem abirim* (Psalm 78:25)</note>, and our Sages, of blessed memory, said: "Hear how many people..." <note>A reference to a passage in the Zohar (II:62b) discussing the Manna</note>. It is well known that the bread they ate in the desert was "bread from heaven." But afterwards, the text says explicitly that the food of the **Companions** is even more elevated and refined than the Manna itself.

The answer to this is that the nourishment is entirely one, drawn from the highest place. However, it is divided in its quality based on the capacity of those receiving it. One recipient might receive it in a more refined state than his fellow, and so it goes for everyone. The explanation is that the world as a whole receives it according to their own coarse nature; for the lower, physical earth, the food is thick and coarse. But it becomes more refined in the "Upper Earth," and the angels receive it according to their nature in the higher heavens, and the **Companions** receive it in their vision <note>The text likely refers to prophetic or mystical "seeing"</note> as it is drawn from the **Higher Wisdom**. Since the nourishment is essentially one, and the refinement of both [the Manna and the scholars' food] is relatively close in level compared to the rest of the world, the author was not concerned about saying they are the same food. Both are refined and distant from the food of the general world, which is thick and coarse in its quality and nature, being the same inside and out. 

Afterward, he explains in detail that even among refined things, there is a distinction, and one is thinner than the other. However, one who examines the book carefully will see it is better to say they are not all from the same single place, but rather each is drawn from its own appropriate place. When he said, "How many people are nourished by it..." <note>original Aramaic: *kama benei nasha mitzanin mineih*</note> and "the food of these **Companions**," it does not mean it is literally the same source. Rather, the intention is to describe the quality of refinement. This is why the Manna descended; and afterwards, he explained that [the scholars' food] is even thinner than it and comes from a more elevated place, closer to that which "rose in the will" of the **Ancient Holy One** <note>term: *Atika Kadisha*—the highest, most hidden aspect of the Divine will</note>.

This is the flow that descends from **Wisdom** to the "Heavens," which is **the Lesser Countenance** <note>term: *Zeir Anpin*—the intermediate Divine attributes that govern the world</note>. From there, after being blessed, he receives power from that blessing and pours out refined food which descends to the angels—this is what descended for Israel in the desert. You, the **Companions**, receive and merit your nourishment directly from that which descends to the heavens, before it is extended and drawn down through the levels of **unfolding** <note>term: *hishtalshelut*—the chain-like process by which Divine light descends into physical reality</note> to the bottom. What Rabbi Shimon said—"How many people are nourished by it..."—refers to the very highest levels. 

Thus, the words are settled without contradiction. Whatever its quality, we learn that there are many types of nourishment, and both lower and upper beings depend on them. There is no doubt they were all dependent on Israel—to decrease or increase the flow—through their prayer and their Torah. For a person must pray for nourishment every day, as it says in the **Zohar**: "All those people of faith need every single day to ask for their nourishment from the Holy One, Blessed be He, and to offer their prayer for it..." <note>original Aramaic: *kol inun benei meimanuta ba'ayin bekhol yoma veyoma lemishal mezoneihon...*</note>. It also says there: "Whoever asks for his food every single day is called a 'man of faith,' a son for whose sake blessings are found above and below." In this way, you will be established according to the verse where the Holy One, Blessed be He, appointed the **Assembly of Israel** <note>term: *Knesset Yisrael*—the spiritual archetype of the Jewish people, often identified with the Shekhinah or Divine Presence</note> over this...

<meta>catchword: and it is said</meta>

<summary>This page discusses the mystical hierarchy of "nourishment," distinguishing between the physical food of the world, the Manna eaten in the desert, and the highly refined spiritual sustenance of Torah scholars. It emphasizes that the flow of this Divine energy is influenced by human prayer and faith.</summary>
<keywords>Manna, spiritual nourishment, Chavrayya, Chokhmah, Zeir Anpin, Atika Kadisha, prayer, faith, Zohar, Divine flow</keywords>

--- Page 73 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the discussion of spiritual nourishment (the "bread of heaven") to the protective boundaries of the soul and the community, interpreting the metaphorical garden of the Song of Songs as the framework of the Torah's commandments.</meta>

# Song of Songs
<note>original: *Shir HaShirim*</note>

# A garden locked, my sister, my bride; a spring locked,
# a fountain sealed:
<note>Song of Songs 4:12</note>

->Again, a song for her, the lowliness of Israel in their poverty<-
->Open and spread out like...<-
<note>The OCR for "כסחל" is unclear; it likely refers to a state of vulnerability or openness in contrast to the "locked" garden.</note>
->The walls, regarding the matters of this world...<-

And if so, it is like **a garden locked** <note>original: *gan na'ul*</note> with walls and a rampart all around, as in "I will hedge it up" and so on. For through the Torah and its commandments <note>term: *Mitzvot*—the 613 religious duties or "deeds" that govern Jewish life</note>, they are locked and hedged in all their affairs: in their eating, their drinking, their actions, and their clothing; also in the house and the field, in plowing, sowing, and planting; also in their business dealings and in all other matters. All of these act like fences and safeguards for Israel, so that they cannot wander outside. This is a general principle for the entire "wall." 

Furthermore, there are specific parts within it, starting with those who occupy themselves with the Torah, who are the leaders of the congregation, its wise and understanding members. Regarding the wise, it says **a spring locked** <note>original: *gal na'ul*</note>. This refers to the places where waters are gathered and stored—a ritual bath <note>term: *mikvah*—a pool of water used for spiritual purification</note> of water. This is a hint to the sages who learn Torah from their teachers; they are like a "locked spring" <note>The word *gal* can mean a wave, a heap, or a spring-fed pool</note> of gathered water. Because the water is protected within it, no shard or wild beast shall enter to drink from the water, or to make it foul or muddy. So too is their Torah preserved and locked within them; no place for the Outer Forces <note>term: *Hekhala* (lit. "Palace"), often used in mystical texts to refer to the "Other Side" or "Husks" of impurity</note> can be found to enter their Torah to make it foul or muddy. They are also like a "plastered cistern that does not lose a drop" <note>A reference to Ethics of the Fathers (Pirkei Avot 2:8), describing a perfect student</note>, for their Torah is preserved in them. 

Similarly, the phrase **fountain sealed** <note>original: *ma'ayan chatum*</note> refers to those sages who are greater than them, who "understand one thing from another" <note>A traditional term for deductive reasoning and deep intuition</note>. They cause rivers and streams of Torah and wisdom to flow from the source of their intellect, which is like a flowing spring. From it, pleasant waters are drawn. It is like a **sealed fountain**, shut and closed; the impurity of the "Palace" and its legions shall not pass through it, for it is totally sealed. 

Regarding the rest of the common people, it says, "Your shoots are an orchard of pomegranates," as it will be explained shortly. 

Alternatively, the entire verse may refer to classes of people according to their fulfillment of the Torah and commandments. Regarding the standing class, **a garden locked**, etc., the intention is the collective of the nation who are "locked and hedged" in the ways of the Torah in all their details, as mentioned above. 

Regarding another class—those who sanctify themselves even in that which is permitted to them—this is **a spring locked**. Here, "spring" <note>original: *gal*</note> is in the sense of a storm or a gate, as in the phrase "the gates were locked" <note>original: *tarokei galei*—an Aramaic expression from the Talmud</note>, as Rashi, of blessed memory, wrote. The meaning is that they close themselves off from the "storm" that entices them regarding worldly matters; they rebuke it and the [unrefined] teachings, and only occupy themselves with what is necessary for the survival of the soul. Regarding this class, it says **a spring locked**. 

There is yet another class that practices ways of abstinence <note>term: *perishut*—the practice of withdrawing from physical pleasures for spiritual growth</note> and piety <note>term: *chasidut*</note to a very great degree. Regarding them, it says **a sealed fountain**. They are like a fountain of living waters; because of their purity, refinement, and sweetness, they are very beautiful and good. So too, the wisdom of this class is of a high level. All of them are closed and shut away from their eyes and visions; they distance themselves as far as a bowshot. Therefore, they have taken upon themselves the yoke of holiness to afflict their physical matter and to subdue their Evil Inclination <note>term: *Yetzer HaRa*—the innate human impulse toward selfishness or sin</note> in order to delight in the will of the service of God in its simplicity. Therefore, he also called them a **sealed fountain**, to indicate the intensity of their abstinence, for "sealed" is more restrictive than "locked."

If you wish, this also refers to the three levels of holiness found in Israel: Israelites, Levites, and Priests <note>original: *Kohanim*</note>. For Levites have an extra holiness beyond Israelites as they guard the [Temple] service and have more commandments than Israelites. The Priests are above them, for they have even more commandments and their holiness ascends very high, as it is written: "They shall be holy [unto their God]" (Leviticus 21:6). They have many additional commandments, as is well known.

<vocab>Song of Songs, locked garden, locked spring, sealed fountain, Israel, Torah, commandments, abstinence, piety, Levites, Priests, holiness, Sages</vocab>

<summary>This page interprets the "locked garden" and "sealed fountain" of Song of Songs as metaphors for the protective boundaries of the Torah and the varying levels of spiritual devotion among the Jewish people. It categorizes these metaphors as representing the general public, the scholarly sages, and the ascetic pious, or alternatively, the social hierarchy of Israelites, Levites, and Priests.</summary>

<keywords>Song of Songs 4:12, Torah, Mitzvot, Geder (Fence), Sages, Perishut (Abstinence), Kohanim (Priests), Levites, spiritual purity, protection from impurity</keywords>

--- Page 74 ---
<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Song of Songs, transitioning from the "locked garden" (symbolizing the sages and the protective fences of the law) to the inner spiritual life and the specific merits of righteous women. It bridges the metaphors of scent and taste (honey/milk) to the spiritual "garments" the soul wears in the afterlife.</meta>

# Commentary

The inner matters shall be **under your tongue** <note>original: *tachat leshonecha*</note> and hidden within you. Do not imagine in your soul that this will cause you any lack in the soul’s delight after death. For with this, the **scent of your perfections** and the garments and clothing of the soul which you shall have in the Garden of Eden <note>term: *Gan Eden*—the spiritual paradise where souls reside after death</note> will be like the **scent of Lebanon** <note>Song of Songs 4:11</note> due to their power, subtlety, and purity. In this way, the soul will benefit from them greatly, just as the soul enjoys a pleasant fragrance, which is a spiritual thing the soul delights in. This path is founded on the words of our Sages, may their memory be a blessing, regarding the verse: **Honey and milk are under your tongue.**

In the Midrash, Rabbi Berachia in the name of Rabbi Helbo said: Just as sixty myriads <note>600,000</note> of male prophets stood for Israel, so too did sixty myriads of female prophets stand for them. Solomon came and publicized them: **Your lips drip nectar, my bride** <note>Song of Songs 4:11</note>. For it is written, **Your lips drip**, using the language of prophecy, as it is written in the book of Micah: **Do not preach [drip], they preach...** <note>Micah 2:6; the Hebrew word for "drip" (*nataph*) is also a technical term for prophesying</note>. It is clear from his words that this verse speaks in praise of righteous and modest women. The intention is to show that it is not only the wise men of the Community of Israel who are worthy of the dwelling of the Divine Name among those below—the reason mentioned regarding the "shorn ones" above—but also the merits of righteous women help to assist this and add to every matter. 

For many women rose to the level of prophecy in those distinguished generations, as explained in the Midrash regarding the verse **Your lips drip**, etc. Indeed, the verse concludes afterwards in praise of righteous women by saying **Honey and milk are under your tongue**, meaning that they are also worthy of praise regarding the matter of the Torah. Even though women are not obligated in the study of Torah <note>term: *Talmud Torah*—the formal obligation to study Jewish law and scripture, traditionally assigned to men</note>, nevertheless, the merit of Torah belongs to them in the manner described in the tractate Berachot <note>referencing Berachot 17a, which asks "How do women gain merit?"</note>: by "waiting for their husbands" to return from study and "taking their children to the study house," etc. Regarding this, it says **honey and milk**, which refers to the study of Torah, **under your tongue**—meaning as if it were under your own tongue, and the portion of women who learn and occupy themselves with God.

The verse concludes by saying the grace and **scent of your perfections**, etc. This is to say that even if, by necessity, righteous women do not have the same opportunity to create the spiritual garments and perfections in which their souls will be clothed in the Garden of Eden—garments and clothes that should be complete without any lack—the Sages were precise in saying that through the performance of the commandments <note>term: *Mitzvot*—the 613 religious deeds</note>, which are hinted at by the 248 limbs <note>traditional Jewish anatomy counts 248 limbs/organs, corresponding to the 248 positive commandments</note>, the spiritual form and body are made for the soul to wear when it separates from the material body it has in this world. 

After one's life, if a person lacks the ability to make their spiritual form complete because some commandments did not come to their hand—such as the commandment of Levirate marriage <note>term: *Yibum*—a specific law where a man marries his childless brother's widow; this only applies in rare circumstances</note>, or the commandments of the Priesthood for one who is not a Priest, or the reverse for a Priest when the Temple is not standing, or the commandments dependent on the Land of Israel for one who lives outside the Land—nonetheless, through the Torah, nothing is missing. One who occupies himself with the laws of the commandments that he cannot physically perform is credited as if he fulfilled them. This is even true for one who is not a scholar, if he supports those who study Torah, as we have explained in another place to prove that the primary repair for this is the Torah. If so, since righteous women are exempt from the obligation of Torah study, it might seem they have no way to repair and complete the garments and form of the soul. Therefore, the text says **honey and milk**, etc., to show they have a connection to the Torah through "supporting their husbands and children." Through this, the repair of their soul is achieved. Since they are the cause for the Torah of their husbands and sons, the merit of that Torah is attributed to them, as if they were perfecting themselves in Torah like the Sages themselves. If so, it is fitting to praise them and say: **the scent of your perfections is like the scent of Lebanon**, regarding their merits and their dripping nectar and all their matters.

# Song of Songs

Similar to this, may their memory be a blessing, is the idea that your "peace-offerings" <note>original: *shlamecha*</note> are your commandments. These are like "orchards" <note>original: *karmonin*</note> and I have compared you to "crushed" olives <note>original: *ketitin*; a reference to the pure oil used in the Temple</note>. We judge the sons of Zak, the righteous ones, and the Kutim <note>Samaritans</note>, and your scent is as pleasant as the spices of the Garden of Eden <note>original: *Dnabta v’Eden*</note>, and so on. Thus, "your shoots" <note>Song of Songs 4:13</note> refers to the branches, which are the young men; "precious fruits" refers to the offspring; and he interpreted their path according to another way.

**Spikenard and saffron, calamus and cinnamon, with all trees of frankincense, myrrh and aloes, with all the chief spices.**

The Ibn Ezra <note>term: *Ibn Ezra*—Abraham ben Meir ibn Ezra (1089–1167), a major Spanish Jewish scholar and biblical commentator</note>, may his memory be a blessing, wrote that there are twelve spices listed here, corresponding to the **twelve tribes** of Israel. He explained that "let it breathe" <note>referring to the spirit of the previous verse</note> is meant to show that the matter of this praise includes all the "gazes" <note>term: *mabatim*—spiritual perspectives or divine influences</note> of God, and that there might be slight differences between them, just as there are different varieties of spices. This is hinted at in the verse: their scent is good, some more than others. Just as there are various types of goodness and scent, these things are arranged like spices, according to our words explained above.

Regarding the mention of the twelve "gazes," one could say that within the collective of the nation, there are many different parts. Some possess the "scent" and "taste" of the Torah and the commandments <note>term: *mitzvot*—religious duties or good deeds</note>. Others might possess only the scent, or only the taste of Torah, or only the commandments. Within the category of "scent," there are various levels, one above the other, as some scents are better or more potent than others; the same applies to "taste." Those who examine the types mentioned in the verses will find some items possess both scent and taste, some only scent, and some only taste. In this way, there is a hint here to all the different parts of the nation, demonstrating the praise of the nation in all its general and specific components. This is for the purpose mentioned above: to silence the angels so they do not prosecute or complain about the Divine Presence <note>term: *Shekhinah*—the dwelling or settling of the divine presence</note> residing among those in the lower world.

**A fountain of gardens, a well of living waters, and flowing streams from Lebanon.**

Here he concludes the "dessert" and the praise of the Ancient Nation <note>referring to Israel</note>, which is arranged as those who guide the masses. They merit the public through their guidance and leadership, bringing them closer to the Torah and good deeds. In this way, pure and clear waters flow from them to the rest of the nation to water them, so they may be like "gardens" in Torah and commandments. The source of their Torah is the laws they hear, learn, and follow to fear the Lord all their days. This is the meaning of: **A fountain of gardens, a well of living waters.**

Since merit is brought to the many, they become like "houses" for the study of Torah and the fulfillment of commandments. Regarding the fulfillment of commandments, he said **a fountain of gardens**, for all the gardens—which are the various parts of the nation—drink and are sustained by that fountain. Regarding the study of Torah, he said **a well of living waters**. He further said **and flowing streams from Lebanon** <note>original: *Levanon*</note> because there are those whose power is in preaching but not in practice. He says here that the Holy One, blessed be He, does not destroy their deeds, and their words should not be like a [failing] spring. He says it is not so; for these waters are "flowing and drawn from Lebanon." This means to say the words flow and drip from those who possess "whiteness" <note>original: *loven*; a wordplay on Lebanon, implying purity</note>. Each of them is whitened by their good deeds; they possess the power to preach and they fulfill their own words, producing fruit. Thus, their rest is great, for the merit of the many depends on them.

It is possible to specify these words as coming from God, blessed be He. He concludes with great praise: that the upper and lower worlds, and all the goodness that flows to all the worlds, depends on those below. He says they are the Ancient Nation, the people He chose as His heritage. And even though this matter was already mentioned above in the verse "How beautiful is your love" and "Your lips drip nectar," we have no problem with the repetition, as it follows other interpretive paths. Even for those...

### Commentary
### 3
<meta>catchword: near</meta>

-> **Your shoots are an orchard of pomegranates with precious** <-
-> **fruits, henna with spikenard.** <-

<column-break/>

According to the first interpretation, the word "your shoots" <note>original: *shlachecha*</note> follows the order of the source of sustenance. The term "your shoots" is derived from the expression for an "irrigated field" <note>term: *beit hashalchin* — in Jewish law and agriculture, a field that is naturally dry and requires constant, manual irrigation to remain fertile</note>, which must be watered continuously. Our Sages, of blessed memory, interpreted "your shoots" as referring to a dry land called an "irrigated field" that must be constantly watered. The intention here is not merely those who perform good deeds or the masters of the Torah—who possess the initial beauty and mercy required for every holy matter, as mentioned above—but rather the "others" who begin to blossom. These are compared to an "orchard of pomegranates." 

As our Sages said: "Even the empty ones among you are full of commandments like a pomegranate." <note>A famous Talmudic teaching from Tractate Berakhot 57a, suggesting that even those who seem secular or "empty" of Torah knowledge often possess a vast number of good deeds.</note> Here, the text is not speaking of those who are entirely empty, but rather those who are like "dry land," meaning they require the "water" and "moisture" of the Torah. These are the people of good character and those who perform righteous actions. Because they are compared to an irrigated field, the verse says they are "with precious fruits," meaning they produce the fruit of good character and good deeds in the world. They support the Torah and study it with a thirst for its "water," possessing both "taste" and "fragrance." This is the meaning of "henna with spikenard." 

It is also possible that "with precious fruits" refers to what our Sages said: "Be careful with the children of the poor, for from them Torah shall go forth to the world." <note>Tractate Nedarim 81a; an injunction to respect the impoverished because intellectual and spiritual leadership often arises from their ranks.</note> Just as water flows to the lowest places, the text here says that the humble among Israel are like an "orchard of pomegranates with precious fruits." The focus is on the fruit that comes from them, in a place where there is Torah and good deeds, as it says: "precious fruits, henna with spikenard."

Alternatively, one can interpret "your shoots" as meaning "branches and boughs." The intention here is the "youth of Israel." Even though the "Evil Inclination" <note>term: *Yetzer HaRa* — the innate human impulse toward selfish or physical desires</note> clings to young men due to the "boiling of their blood"—for youth and adolescence bring much impulsiveness—nonetheless, the youth of Israel have a path for the spirit to enter them <unclear>and like a spring and a horse</unclear> <note>The Hebrew here is difficult to decipher; it likely compares the vigor of youth to a rushing stream or a galloping horse.</note> through the grace of the Torah of the Lord, its ways, its boundaries, and its safeguards. Because they were introduced to these measures early on and were raised in the purity and holiness of the Torah, their physical desires and material nature do not lead them into disgrace and sin like other nations. Those other nations have not seen the light; they are raised in impurity, lacking any spiritual protection, and are bent and enslaved under the shell of their impulses. To the extent that they are rebellious, the power of their better inclination is diminished.

Therefore, "your shoots" are an "orchard of pomegranates." This means they are like the pomegranate, which is a very choice fruit; its inner part is much greater than its peel, and even its peel has use and benefit, as is well known. Furthermore, they are compared to an orchard of pomegranates because the pomegranate fruit is not only pure and choice, but also contains a hint of "virtue" <note>original: *chayil*</note> in its interior, serving as a lesson on the humility and submission of the youth. Even as they flourish and sprout in the strength of their youth and power, this becomes a great opening for them to subdue the Evil Inclination, for they are "lovely youths, a seed blessed by the Lord."

Additionally, what follows from them—their offspring born during their youth—is a "kosher seed." This is because their intention in marriage is not for the sake of lustful desire, but rather for the sake of Heaven and in observance of the laws of the Torah regarding life with one's wife. Therefore, holiness rests upon their offspring. Even if a small amount of physical desire is mixed in, it is nullified by the majority of their holy intent <unclear>small gift</unclear> and does not disqualify the "holy offerings." This is the meaning of "with precious fruits."

Regarding the phrase "henna with spikenard," this refers to the fruit that comes from them, which possesses two qualities: it is disciplined in the matter of sexual desire—which is "henna" <note>original: *kopher*</note>, because it is the nature of henna <note>The commentator here plays on the word *kopher* (henna) and *likpor* (to deny/break), suggesting it breaks the power of lust.</note> to cut off this evil desire—and furthermore, it gives off a "good scent" due to their good deeds, which is "with spikenard." It is possible to say that "henna with spikenard" refers to the "shoots" (the young men) themselves, and it all follows one consistent path. And I saw that the Translator <note>likely referring to the Aramaic Targum</note> translated it as:
near

# Song of Songs

In all these particulars, I have publicized that I have experienced [divine] mercy. "I have gathered my myrrh" <note>original: *ariti mori* — quoting Song of Songs 5:1</note> refers to the confession of those who trust in the materials of **Torah study** in which Israel engages, for through it we are delighted and refined, as it were, by the breath of the mouths of those who engage in it. Since the study of Torah is a thin and refined matter, it also provides a spiritual spirit to the souls, which is inherited through the spirit alone, and it is a very subtle thing. 

Regarding the **performance of commandments**, the verse says: "I have eaten my honeycomb with my honey." Regarding the **offering of sacrifices**, it says: "I have drunk my wine with my milk." Even the angels "ate," as it says: "Eat, O friends; drink, and be drunk, O beloveds," for the overflow of goodness and blessing is also drawn to them by virtue of the aforementioned things, as explained previously. 

The verse says "beloveds" and "friends" to refer to the two classes of angels, or perhaps it refers to every single class, as there are levels within them, one above the other. Thus, they differ in their pleasure—one "eating" and another "drinking." My master <note>likely referring to a specific teacher or a prominent commentator like Maimonides or the Arizal</note> explained that all these are referred to as "to their world" <note>unclear: likely referring to the spiritual realms</note> because in the existence of everything, one requires divine assistance to achieve a deed. In the matter of "eating," it is difficult for a person to reach the end of it, but "one who comes to be purified is helped." Therefore, "my master" said that these things come with divine assistance. Nevertheless, the verse says "I have gathered" to show that He, blessed be He, desires and longs for them and takes pleasure in them. 

The **Targum** <note>term: *Targum* — the ancient Aramaic translation and paraphrase of the Hebrew Bible, which often adds layers of homiletic interpretation</note> interprets all of this as referring to the Holy Temple, its sacrifices, and its service. "I have gathered my myrrh" refers to the incense; "I have eaten my honeycomb" refers to the meat of the burnt offerings and sacrifices; "I have drunk my wine with my milk" refers to the libations of water and wine. "Eat, O friends" refers to the priests who eat the sacrifices that belong to them from the table of the Lord. These words are consistent with our approach here, and his words are clear.

---

-> After the praise heard from His mouth, blessed be He <-

**I sleep, but my heart is awake; the voice of my beloved** refers to the indwelling of the **Divine Presence** <note>term: *Shekhinah* — the feminine aspect of God that dwells within the physical world, particularly in the Temple</note> among the lower beings. **Knocking: "Open to me, my sister, my darling,"** which happened in practice when the glory of the Lord dwelt in the **Holy Temple**, as discussed above. 

Now the text recounts that while the House of Israel sat upon their land, with the Temple on its site, the priests at their service, the Levites at their song and music, and Israel at their stations—the hand of impurity grew strong. They turned away from following the Lord and began to sin, particularly in the worship of other gods. They abandoned the "fountain of living waters" to hew for themselves "broken cisterns," as is well-known from the words of the Prophets. 

The Holy One, blessed be He, waited and sent warnings to them by the hand of every prophet and every seer, that they should turn from their evil ways and return to the Lord their God, lest the wrath of the Lord be kindled and He destroy the Holy Temple, and they be exiled like water from their Father's table. 

This is the meaning of **"I sleep, but my heart is awake."** This refers to the Assembly of Israel saying: "Even though I am asleep"—for sleep is compared to the nullification of the power of the senses—it is known that the Lord makes a person "die" in sleep <note>a reference to the Jewish idea that sleep is 1/60th of death</note>. Simply put, this refers to "sleep" from the service of Heaven and the fulfillment of the Torah. As our Sages of blessed memory said: "I am asleep" regarding the commandments, "but my heart is awake" regarding the Holy Temple, which is called the "heart" <note>note: This interpretation is found in *Midrash Rabbah* on Song of Songs</note>. 

As they expounded in the Midrash on Lamentations regarding the verse "this is your knowledge, for it is bitter, for it has reached your heart," this means that while the Temple was on its site and the service was maintained, the rebuke of the prophets was constant, warning them until the time and season when He would no longer "slumber nor sleep." The Holy Temple "slept" from its "table" <note>metaphor for the cessation of sacrifices</note>. **"The voice of my beloved knocks"** is the voice of the Holy One, blessed be He, through the prophets and messengers, for His words were with strength and awe, and He desired greatly to warn them because the time of the "table" and their sin would not...

It then explains "I sleep, but my heart is awake" as a critique of Israel's spiritual negligence during the First Temple period despite the "knocking" (warnings) of the prophets.</summary>

### Commentary

These matters were stated in brief, and here the explanation is expanded. The verse says, "**A fountain of gardens**" <note>original: *ma’yan ganim* — Song of Songs 4:15</note> as a seal, to say: Behold, there is a fountain here to water all the gardens and all the worlds, and it is divided into a "fountain" and "**a well of living waters**" <note>original: *be’er mayim hayim*</note>. This means that from you (the Assembly of Israel) flow the living waters, which is the spiritual and refined abundance (*shefa*) for the place where this vow is fulfilled. Even for the drawing down of the aforementioned abundance from "**Lebanon**" <note>original: *Levanon* — literally "whiteness," interpreted here as the source of pure Mercy</note>, which is the place of the **Supreme Whiteness** <note>original: *loven elyon*</note>, the attribute of **Mercy** (*Hesed*) high above in the upper lights—there too, everything is blessed and becomes a source of blessing through the power of Divine Compassion, so that my dwelling may be established. Therefore, since you are the source of goodness for all worlds—or perhaps the verse means "a fountain of gardens" because all the higher levels, lights, and delights of the **Garden of Eden**, both upper and lower, are drawn out, multiplied, and sustained from this fountain and source through the fulfillment of the **Torah and its commandments**. This is similar to the concept that "the reward for a commandment is the commandment itself." This is why it is called a "fountain of gardens," referring to all gardens, lower and upper. Afterward, it is called "a well of living waters" regarding the preparation for the **World to Come**. It explains that from them flow "streams from Lebanon," which refers to the delight of the abundance that flows from your very self, for you are the source from the place of the Supreme Whiteness.

-> Behold, in the wisdom of Solomon in his holy spirit, <-
-> He concluded the words of the Blessed Name regarding the <-
-> Assembly of Israel and how He agreed to her words to establish <-
-> His dwelling among the lower beings through proven arguments: <-
-> **Awake, O north wind, and come, O south wind!** <-
-> **Blow upon my garden, that its spices may flow out.** <-
-> **Let my beloved come to his garden, and eat its choicest fruits.** <-
<note>original: quoting Song of Songs 4:16</note>

He opened his mouth toward the "kings" <note>likely referring to the angelic ministers or the spiritual forces governing the nations</note> and said to them: Whether the kings are from the side of **Judgment** (*Din*) and the "Left"—metaphorically called the "**North**" <note>original: *tzafon*</note> and who are more prone to act as accusers—or the kings from the side of the "Right," the attribute of **Mercy** (*Hesed*), metaphorically called the "**South**" <note>original: *teman*</note>, who are naturally inclined toward mercy—it is distant for you to teach and agree upon this matter. Not only should you not act as accusers, but on the contrary, I expect each of your groups to "blow upon my garden" so that through your spirit, my garden—which is the **Holy Temple** <note>original: *Beit HaMikdash*</note>—may be sustained. Through your good spirit, may it be that "my beloved comes to his garden," which is the Holy Temple, to dwell among the lower beings. And "let him eat his choicest fruits," which are pleasant and sweet to Him according to His word. This refers to the **Torah**, the **commandments**, and the **good deeds** found within Israel, or to the **sacrifices** and the various types of service within the Holy Temple.

As I have written, this reconciles why the verse says "Awake" for the North and "Come" for the South. The kings from the side of the "Left" (the North) require an "awakening" because they are naturally distant from this matter due to their character and temperament, which is to accuse and oppose. However, the kings from the side of the "Right" are naturally close to it because of their righteousness, and for them, a simple call to "come" is sufficient. It is also possible that these are the words of God Himself to the kings regarding this intention. And when we say, "Let my beloved come to his garden," it follows the path I have described.

<meta>The previous page discussed the Assembly of Israel as a "fountain of gardens," explaining how the performance of commandments draws Divine abundance (shefa) from the highest realms of Mercy into the world. This page continues the commentary on Song of Songs Chapter 5, shifting from the invitation of the Beloved to the specific titles given to the Assembly of Israel and the consequences of the Divine Presence withdrawing from the Temple.</meta>

# Song of Songs

...night. And now, since this level of attachment <note>Hebrew: *dveikut* — a mystical state of cleaving to the Divine</note> has departed from the revealed world, it results in a great Desecration of the Name <note>Hebrew: *Hillul Hashem*</note>. Therefore, the verse says, "Open to me..." etc. The reason He uses these four specific names <note>"My sister, my love, my dove, my perfect one" — Song of Songs 5:2</note> is perhaps because the attachment of the "Wall" <note>An allegorical reference to the Assembly of Israel or the spiritual protection of the Temple</note> is withdrawn in four stages and for four reasons.

The first relates to the essential closeness that cannot be truly distanced; for this, He calls her "**my sister**" <note>Hebrew: *ahoti*</note>. The second refers to the attachment through the Torah and the commandments, by which all the worlds are sustained. Through this, the "Wall" rises to the level of "**my love**" <note>Hebrew: *ra’yati*</note>, for God is the grace of the world and she sustains it. This is like the saying of the sages: "as a crown that graces the beloved." The third is the perfection of attachment where the people belong to God and God to them without any substitution; regarding this, He says "**my dove**" <note>Hebrew: *yonati*</note> and "**my perfect one**" <note>Hebrew: *tamati*</note>. As the sages taught: just as a dove recognizes its mate and never exchanges it for another. "**My love**" also refers to the Divine Presence <note>Hebrew: *Shekhinah*</note> dwelling among the lower realms within the Children of Israel by means of the Holy Temple. This is the highest possible perfection of attachment achievable in this world, which is why she is called "**my perfect one**," from the language of "perfection" <note>Hebrew: *shlemut*</note>.

These three levels are known to all. But regarding the "Walls of the World," how can they be recognized? Without doubt, when Israel is despised and lowly, it is a day of great Desecration of the Name, for they are attached to Him. According to the strength and power of their bond of attachment, so too increases the weight of the Desecration of the Name. Therefore, He mentions all these titles upon His return.

Now, my intention is to explain these verses in the context of the time when the Holy Temple stood. I know it is possible to interpret them regarding the Destruction and the exile of Israel among the nations, following the path of the Midrash <note>Ancient rabbinic homiletic interpretations</note>. They taught: "I am asleep" regarding the sacrifices, "but my heart is awake" for the Shema and prayer; "I am asleep" regarding the Temple, "but my heart is awake" for the synagogues and study halls. However, I have chosen this "distant path" because it follows the consistent method we have taken in choosing the general theme of the Temple service, even if it seems a further reach.

---

->**I have put off my coat; how shall I put it on?**<-
->**I have washed my feet; how shall I soil them?**<-
<note>Song of Songs 5:3</note>

---

It is possible that this is a continuation of God's words as He returns to the "Wall." The meaning is: "For your sake, I have put off **my coat**," which refers to the Upper World, the World of Kings. This realm is called a "garment" <note>Hebrew: *malbush*</note> and is a lesser degree compared to the height of pure spiritual attachment. I left it and established my main dwelling among the lower beings through the Holy Temple. And now, how can I put on that first garment again? That is, how can I withdraw back to the heights because of your evil deeds?

"**I have washed my feet**"—these are the parts of the lower world, as it is written, "And the earth is my footstool" <note>Isaiah 66:1</note>. For I purified them and refined them by dwelling within them. And now, "how shall I soil them" by my departure from them? For if I leave, they will return to their original lowly state and be "soiled" by the destructive power of impurity <note>Hebrew: *tum'ah*</note>, just as it was before the Divine Presence descended to the lower realms. According to this explanation, "**my feet**" refers to that which is near the "feet"—meaning the lower physical world. Thus, His words are the plea of one who entreats and returns in repentance, lamenting the evil that will befall the lower worlds, as they will be left exposed to harm. It is as if this matter is very difficult in His eyes.

<vocab>Song of Songs, attachment, Holy Temple, Upper World, Lower World, Divine Presence, Midrash</vocab>

<summary>This page provides a mystical commentary on Song of Songs 5:2-3, interpreting the titles "sister, love, dove, and perfect one" as levels of spiritual attachment. It explains the "coat" and "feet" of God as metaphors for the Upper and Lower worlds, framing the Divine departure from the Temple as a tragic necessity caused by human actions.</summary>
<keywords>Song of Songs, Dveikut (Attachment), Hillul Hashem (Desecration), Shekhinah, Holy Temple, impurity (tum'ah), Midrashic interpretation, Divine Presence</keywords>

<meta>The previous page discussed the Assembly of Israel as a "fountain of gardens," explaining how the performance of commandments draws Divine abundance (shefa) from the highest realms of Mercy into the world. This page continues the commentary on Song of Songs Chapter 5, shifting from the invitation of the Beloved to the specific titles given to the Assembly of Israel and the consequences of the Divine Presence withdrawing from the Temple.</meta>

# Commentary

Their worth is diminished by their sins, but if they return in repentance <note>Hebrew: *Teshuvah* — literally "returning" to God</note>, their evil deeds will be as if they never existed. Furthermore, they will not require great toil, only an awakening and a beginning, and they will receive assistance from Heaven to reach the final goal. This is what is meant by the verse "Open to me..." and as our Sages of blessed memory said in the Midrash <note>A genre of ancient rabbinic interpretation</note>: Rabbi Yissa said, the Holy One, blessed be He, said to Israel: "My children, open for me one opening of repentance like the eye of a needle, and I will open for you openings through which wagons and carriages can enter." 

It is possible that he was precise with the word "**to me**" <note>Hebrew: *Li*</note>, which seems redundant, to hint that the opening should be according to the attribute of His goodness, may He be blessed. It is known that "one who comes to be purified is assisted." He also hinted with the word "**to me**" that the opening should be like His own aspect—which is not a body nor a physical force, but rather the ultimate subtle spirituality. Such spirituality is not hindered by the smallness of an entrance, and even the smallest drop or particle will suffice. This is easy to understand.

As for what He said, "**my sister, my love, my dove, my perfect one**," our Sages of blessed memory have already explained these four names in the Midrash:
*   "**My sister**" <note>Hebrew: *Ahoti*</note> — for they became attached to Him in Egypt through two commandments: the blood of the Passover offering and the blood of circumcision.
*   "**My love**" <note>Hebrew: *Ra'yati*</note> — for they "pastured" Me <note>A wordplay on the Hebrew root R-A-H, meaning both friend/beloved and to shepherd/graze</note> at the Sea and said, "This is my God and I will glorify Him."
*   "**My dove**" <note>Hebrew: *Yonati*</note> — at Marah, where they were commanded all the laws, details, and good reasons, like a dove that is distinguished by its faithfulness.
*   "**My perfect one**" <note>Hebrew: *Tamati*</note> — for they became "perfected" with Me at Sinai and said, "All that the Lord has spoken we will do and we will hear."

This fits our path perfectly, for the merits of the ancestors and the kindness of the early generations represented by these four names assist in accepting them in repentance through a mere awakening of the heart. Even the simplest will is enough, for based on what happened in Egypt, at the Sea, at Marah, and at Sinai, it is fitting that they should not be replaced by another nation. Furthermore, it is possible according to our path that He indicated that even now, she <note>The Assembly of Israel</note> is called by her original names. She does not lose her status because of her sins or her ways, for when they return in repentance, the crooked will be made straight. Her youth will be renewed like an eagle's, and her former hidden bundles of sin will not be remembered.

And when He said, "**for my head is filled with dew**," the intention is that the pure person is awakened very quickly to return in repentance. This is because despite all His mercies, may He be blessed, by which He used to forgive them in the past, now the forces of Judgment <note>Hebrew: *Din* — the cosmic attribute of justice and severity</note> have begun to prosecute and stir up troubles and exile <note>Hebrew: *Golut*</note> against Israel. 

This is the secret of "**my head is filled with dew**"—meaning, although in the past my head was filled with "dew" (referring to the great abundance and the dews of blessing), now, because of your evil deeds, "**my locks**" are beginning to drip "**the drops of the night**." This refers to the forces of Judgment hinted at by the word "locks" <note>In Kabbalah, the "locks of hair" represent the outpourings of Divine judgment</note>, which send forth judgment and drip the drops of the "night"—the night being a known metaphor for exile.

Alternatively, "**Open to me**" intends to show that a person must always keep before their eyes His Name, which is being profaned among the nations. This is the primary direction toward which one should turn and look: to feel for and pity the honor of His service. If, God forbid, one has already strayed, one should move quickly to repair it. It is clear that while they are exiled from their land and the Temple is destroyed, a great Desecration of the Name <note>Hebrew: *Hillul Hashem*</note> is sustained by their suffering, as they are taunted all day long. The weight of the sin of Desecration of the Name among those who delay the end of the destruction and exile is a very great sin. 

Therefore, "**Open to me**" means for the sake of My Name, so to speak, I am awakened for good. The primary intention should be for Him and for His sake. This is the secret of "**Open to me**"—for My glory and for My sake, so that My glory shall not be profaned. For since you are Mine, My portion and My treasure, My glory is found in your peace, and in your suffering, My glory is profaned. This is the secret of "**my sister, my love**," etc. Since they truly "pastured" Him with kindness, they are influenced by and draw down all good things and dews of blessing—as if to say "**my head is filled with dew**." But God forbid, when kindness is turned the other way by the yoke of suffering and exile, it leads to His great profanation. This is the secret of "**my locks with the drops of the night**."

<summary>This page interprets Song of Songs 5:2 as a Divine call to repentance, explaining that even a small effort from Israel triggers massive Divine aid. It correlates the four titles given to Israel with historical merits (Egypt, Red Sea, Marah, Sinai) and contrasts "dew" as Divine mercy with "drops of night" as the hardships of exile and the judgment of God.</summary>
